\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\frac{1}{r^{3}}\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right)\right]r+3\left(\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\frac{1}{r^{3}}\right)\right]r\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+3\left(\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\frac{1}{r^{3}}\right)\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+3\left(\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{-3\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+3\left(\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

therefore

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}\ +{\rm i}\,g\Bigl(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{A}}\Bigr)=0

(198)

is automatically satisfied. Namely, Eq. (
11c
) has no conditions for two functions
f_{1}(r)
and
f_{2}(r)
.

Analysis 3: The study of Eq. (
11b
)—
Since
\vec{\mathcal{B}}
is time-independent, then Eq. (
11b
) becomes

\displaystyle-\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{B}}\Bigr]-\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}-\vec{\mathcal{E}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\biggr)=0.

(199)

Let us study the
z
-component of Eq. (
199
), which is

\displaystyle-\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ {\mathcal{B}}_{z}\Bigr]-\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}-\left(\vec{\mathcal{E}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}\biggr)=0.

(200)

One can have

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,\ {\mathcal{B}}_{z}\right]=\left[f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{2}(r),\ -(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}z\right]=0.

(201)

and

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}=\dfrac{\partial\mathcal{E}_{y}}{\partial x}-\dfrac{\partial\mathcal{E}_{x}}{\partial y}

(202)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left[-\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}y+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)S_{y}\right]

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left[-\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}x+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)S_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}y+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left\{\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)\right\}S_{y}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}x-\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left\{\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)\right\}S_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\dfrac{\partial\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{\partial x}+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\right)\right\}y

\displaystyle+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{x}{r}S_{y}

\displaystyle+\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\dfrac{\partial\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{\partial y}+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\right)\right\}x

\displaystyle-\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{y}{r}S_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\dfrac{x}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)S_{x}+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\right)\dfrac{x}{r}\right\}y

\displaystyle+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{x}{r}S_{y}

\displaystyle+\left\{\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\right]\dfrac{y}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)S_{y}+\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\right)\dfrac{y}{r}\right\}x

\displaystyle-\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{y}{r}S_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right)\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}.

Furthermore, one has

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}\right)\times\left[{-}\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}\vec{r}+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)\vec{S}\right]

(203)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{r}\times\vec{S})\times\vec{r}\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{r}\times\vec{S})\times\vec{S}\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{S}-\vec{r}(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{S}\times\vec{r})\times\vec{S}\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}\vec{S}-\vec{r}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{S})\vec{r}-\vec{S}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r),

thus

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}-z(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}

(204)

\displaystyle+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{S})z-S_{z}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r).

Since
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
and
\vec{\mathcal{E}}
are hermitian, so

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\mathcal{E}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}\right\}^{\dagger}=\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}-z(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]

(205)

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{S})z-(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})S_{z}\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r).

Therefore

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}+\left(\vec{\mathcal{E}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}

(206)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[-S_{z}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle+\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}\right]+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})S_{z}\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}\right]\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[-S_{z}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle+\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[r^{2}{S}_{z}\right]+\dfrac{k}{r^{2}}\left[(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})S_{z}\right]\left[1-g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right\}\left[{S}_{z}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right]-k\dfrac{1-g\hbar k}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\left[S_{z}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]

\displaystyle+k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\right\}\left[\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right){S}_{z}\right]+k\dfrac{1-g\hbar k}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\left[(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}){S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left[{S}_{z}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right]+k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left[\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right){S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left[{S}_{z},\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left[{S}_{z},xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left({\rm i}\hbar xS_{y}-{\rm i}\hbar yS_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}.

Based on which we have

\displaystyle-\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ {\mathcal{B}}_{z}\Bigr]-\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{E}}\right)_{z}-\left(\vec{\mathcal{E}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}\biggr)

(207)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-g\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}{-{\rm i}\,g\left[-{\rm i}\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{-2g\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}}.

To satisfy the
z
-component of Eq. (
11b
), we should have the following condition

g\hbar k\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right\}=0,

(208)

i.e.,

g\hbar k=0,

(209)

or

\frac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)=0.

(210)

The latter one leads to

f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r}.

(211)

Similarly, one can prove that the
x
-component and the
y
-component of Eq. (
11b
) lead to the same condition.

Analysis 4: The study of Eq. (
11d
)—
Since
\vec{\mathcal{E}}
is time-independent, then Eq. (
11d
) becomes

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr]+\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}+\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\biggr)=0,

(212)

Let us study the
z
-component of Eq. (
212
), which is

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ {\mathcal{E}}_{z}\Bigr]+\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}+\left(\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}\biggr)=0.

(213)

One can have

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{E}}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,-\left\{\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\partial f_{2}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+f_{1}(r)\dfrac{g\hbar k}{r^{2}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\right\}z+\left[-1+g\hbar k\right]f_{1}(r){S}_{z}\right]

(214)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-1+g\hbar k\right)f_{1}(r)\left[\varphi,{S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-1+g\hbar k\right)f_{1}(r)\left[f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{2}(r),{S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-1+g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},{S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-1+g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)\left[xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z},{S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-1+g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)\left[(-{\rm i}\hbar)xS_{y}+({\rm i}\hbar)yS_{x},{S}_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle({\rm i}\hbar)\left(1-g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}.

Because

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}\right)\times\left[-(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\vec{r}\right]

(215)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{6}}\left[\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)\times\vec{r}\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{6}}\left[(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{S}-\vec{r}(\vec{S}\cdot\vec{r})\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\left[\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\vec{S}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)-\dfrac{1}{r^{6}}\vec{r}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})^{2}\right],

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)^{\dagger}=(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\left[\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\vec{S}-\dfrac{1}{r^{6}}\vec{r}(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})^{2}\right],

(216)

thus

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}+\left(\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}=(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},S_{z}\right]=({\rm i}\hbar)(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}.

(217)

One can have

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}=\dfrac{\partial\mathcal{B}_{y}}{\partial x}-\dfrac{\partial\mathcal{B}_{x}}{\partial y}

(218)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left[-(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}y\right]-\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left[-(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}x\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left[\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right]y+(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left[\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\right]x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\left[\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)y+\dfrac{\partial\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{\partial x}\frac{1}{r^{4}}y\right\}+(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\left[\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)x+\dfrac{\partial\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{\partial y}\frac{1}{r^{4}}x\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left[\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\dfrac{x}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)y+S_{x}\frac{1}{r^{4}}y\right\}+(2-g\hbar k)k\left\{\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\left[\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\dfrac{y}{r}\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)x+S_{y}\frac{1}{r^{4}}x\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}.

Consequently, one has

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ {\mathcal{E}}_{z}\Bigr]+\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{z}+\left(\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)_{z}\biggr)

(219)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}+{\rm i}\,g\left\{({\rm i}\hbar)\left(1-g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}+({\rm i}\hbar)(2-g\hbar k)k^{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-g\hbar\left(1-g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}-g\hbar k(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-g\hbar\left(1-g\hbar k\right)f^{2}_{1}(r)+\left(1-g\hbar k\right)(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-g\hbar k\right)\left[-g\hbar f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z},

and generally

\displaystyle\left(\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)+{\rm i}\,g\biggl(\Bigl[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{E}}\Bigr]+\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right)+\left(\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)\biggr)=\left(1-g\hbar k\right)\left[-g\hbar f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\frac{1}{r^{4}}\right]\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right).

(220)

Therefore, Eq. (
11d
) leads to the following condition

\displaystyle\left(1-g\hbar k\right)\left[-g\hbar f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\right]=0.

(221)

Based on Eq. (
IV.1
), Eq. (
IV.1
), Eq. (
208
), and Eq. (
221
), we have the total conditions for
f_{1}(r)
and
f_{2}(r)
as follows:

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\kappa_{1}r^{\nu_{1}}+\kappa_{2}r^{\nu_{2}},\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a-\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a+\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=2(g\hbar k)^{2},\\
&\left(1-g\hbar k\right)\left[-g\hbar f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\right]=0,\\
&g\hbar k\left[\dfrac{\partial f_{1}(r)}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r}+\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}f_{1}(r)\right]=0.\end{cases}

(222)

The solutions are divided into the following four cases:

Case (i):
g=0
and
k=0
. In this case, we have

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\kappa_{1}r^{\nu_{1}}+\kappa_{2}r^{\nu_{2}}=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}+\kappa_{2},\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a-\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right)=-3,\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a+\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right)=0,\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=0.\end{cases}

(223)

Case (ii):
g=0
and
k\neq 0
. In this case, there are no solutions of
f_{1}(r)
and
f_{2}(r)
for (
222
).

Case (iii):
g\neq 0
and
k=0
. In this case, we have

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=0,\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4}.\end{cases}

(224)

Case (iv):
g\neq 0
,
k\neq 0
. In this case, we have

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\kappa_{1}r^{\nu_{1}}+\kappa_{2}r^{\nu_{2}},\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a-\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a+\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=2(g\hbar k)^{2},\\
&\left(1-g\hbar k\right)=0,\\
&f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r}.\end{cases}

(225)

or

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\kappa_{1}r^{\nu_{1}}+\kappa_{2}r^{\nu_{2}},\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a-\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a+\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right),\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=2(g\hbar k)^{2},\\
&\left[-g\hbar f^{2}_{1}(r)+(2-g\hbar k)k\dfrac{1}{r^{4}}\right]=0,\\
&f_{1}(r)=\frac{C_{1}}{r}.\end{cases}

(226)

The former leads to

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{2}}{r},\;\;\;\kappa_{1}=0,\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=-2,\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=-1,\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=2,\\
&k=\dfrac{1}{g\hbar},\end{cases}

(227)

and the latter leads to

\begin{cases}&f_{1}(r)=\pm\sqrt{\dfrac{(2-g\hbar k)k}{g\hbar}}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}=\pm\dfrac{1}{r^{2}},\\
&f_{2}(r)=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}+\kappa_{4},\\
&\nu_{1}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a-\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right)=-2,\;\;\;\;\nu_{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(1-a+\sqrt{(a-1)^{2}-4b}\right)=0,\\
&a=4,\;\;\;\;b=2(g\hbar k)^{2}=2,\;\;\;g\hbar k=1,\\
&f_{1}(r)=\dfrac{C_{1}}{r},\end{cases}

(228)

which has contradictions, thus there is no solution for
f_{1}(r)
.

From
“Analysis 1”
to
“Analysis 4”
, we have not used the relation
(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S})^{2}=(r^{2}/4)\hbar^{2}
, which is satisfied by spin-
1/2
particle, instead, we have merely used the definition of angular momentum operator, i.e.,
[S_{a},S_{b}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\varepsilon_{abc}S_{c}
. Thus the result shown in Eq. (
163a
) and Eq. (
163b
) is also valid for any spin-
s
angular momentum
\vec{S}
. Furthermore, such a result is also valid for other types of angular momentum operator, such as the isospin and the orbital angular momentum. In conclusion, the following vector spin potential and the scalar potential

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\;\;\varphi=f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{2}(r),

(229)

are the solutions of Yang-Mills equations, where
f_{1}(r)
and
f_{2}(r)
are given in Table
1
. This ends the proof.
∎

Remark 5
.

For the spin operator
\vec{S}
with higher-spin value, if the vector potential is fixed as
\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}
, then the more general form of the scalar potential could be

\displaystyle\varphi=\sum_{j=0}^{2s}f_{j}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)^{j}.

(230)

For example, for the spin-1 case we have

\displaystyle\varphi=\sum_{j=0}^{2}f_{j}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)^{j}=f_{2}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)^{2}+f_{1}(r)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+f_{0}(r),

(231)

one can use the relation
\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)^{3}=\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)
to reduce any high-order term
(such as
\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)^{n}
) to the lower-order terms. However, in this work, for simplicity we restrict our study to the
case of spin-
1/2
.
\blacksquare

Remark 6
.

Let us look at Table
1
. We may know that the case (ii) corresponds to the usual Coulomb-type potential and the
case (iii) has no solutions. In other words, only the solutions of case (i) and case (iv) are spin-dependent potentials.
For convenience, we would like to call the solutions in cases (i) and (iv) as the type-I spin-dependent Coulomb potential and the
type-II spin-dependent Coulomb potential, respectively. Explicitly, the type-I spin-dependent Coulomb potential is give by
(with
g=0,k=0
, and
\kappa_{4}=0
)

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0,

\displaystyle\varphi=\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}+\kappa_{2}\right)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}=\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r},

(232)

with
\hat{r}=\vec{r}/r
. And the type-II spin-dependent Coulomb potential is give by (with
g\neq 0,k\neq 0
,
g\hbar k=1
, and
\kappa_{4}=0
)

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},

\displaystyle\varphi=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}\,\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}=\kappa_{1}\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(233)

For the type-I case, because
[\mathcal{A}_{i},\mathcal{A}_{j}]=0
and
[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]=0
, thus the spin potentials
are Abelian. However, for the type-II case, because
[\mathcal{A}_{i},\mathcal{A}_{j}]\neq 0
and
[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]\neq 0
,
thus the spin potentials are non-Abelian.

\blacksquare

Remark 7
.

Here we would like to clarify some “terms”: The Coulomb potential, the Coulomb-type potential, the spin-dependent Coulomb-type potential, the Coulomb interaction, the spin-dependent Coulomb-type interaction, the spin-dependent Coulomb interaction.

•

The Coulomb potential is usually referred to the standard Coulomb potential in electromagnetics. Mathematically, it is represented by

\displaystyle V(r)=\dfrac{qQ}{r},

(234)

where
q
is the electric charge of particle 1,
Q
is the electric charge of particle 2, and
r
is distance between these two particles.

•

The Coulomb-type potential is referred to the scalar potential
\varphi
, which is a solution of Maxwell’s equations. Mathematically, the Coulomb-type potential is represented by

\displaystyle\varphi=\dfrac{\kappa}{r},

(235)

with
\kappa
being a certain constant. The significant feature of
\varphi
is that it is proportional to
\dfrac{1}{r}
. One may notice that
\varphi
and
V(r)
share the similar form. The relationship between
V(r)
and
\varphi
is as follows

\displaystyle V(r)=q\varphi,

(236)

where
\kappa=Q
is the electric charge of particle 2, and
q
is the electric charge of particle 1. Namely, particle 2 induces a
scalar potential
\varphi=\dfrac{Q}{r}
, when one multiplies (or couples ) the charge
q
(of particle 1) to
the scalar potential
\varphi
(induced by particle 2), then he obtains the Coulomb potential
V(r)
between these two particles.

•

The spin-dependent Coulomb-type potential is referred to a solution of the Yang-Mills equations, which is the generalization
of Coulomb-type potential inherent in Maxwell’s equations. Mathematically, The spin-dependent Coulomb-type potential is represented
simultaneously by the set
\{\vec{\mathcal{A}},\varphi\}
, not solely by
\varphi
. In our work, there are two types of spin-dependent
Coulomb-type potentials. The type-I case corresponds to Eq. (
6
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\{\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0,\;\;\;\varphi=\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right\},

(237)

and the type-II case corresponds to Eq. (
6
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\{\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\varphi=\kappa_{1}\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right\}.

(238)

If we neglect the spin terms, then the spin-dependent Coulomb-type potential reduces to the Coulomb-type potential, i.e.,

\left\{\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0,\varphi=\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right\}
.

•

The Coulomb interaction is referred to a kind of fundamental interactions between two charged particles.
In classical physics, it is characterized by the Coulomb potential
V(r)=\dfrac{qQ}{r}
or the Coulomb force

\vec{F}=\dfrac{qQ}{r^{2}}\,\hat{e}_{r}
. In quantum mechanics, it is characterized by the Coulomb potential

V(r)=\dfrac{qQ}{r}
or the Hamiltonian containing the Coulomb potential. In the non-relativistic quantum mechanics,
the Coulomb interaction is described through the Schrödinger-type Hamiltonian as follows

\mathcal{H}=\frac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+V(r)=\frac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+q\varphi=\frac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+\frac{q\kappa}{r}.

(239)

In the relativistic quantum mechanics, the Coulomb interaction is described through the Dirac-type Hamiltonian as follows

\mathcal{H}=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi=\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta M+\frac{q\kappa}{r},

(240)

where
\vec{\alpha}
and
\beta
are the well-known Dirac’s matrices, i.e.,

\vec{\alpha}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\vec{\sigma}\\
\vec{\sigma}&0\\
\end{array}\right)=\sigma_{x}\otimes\vec{\sigma},\;\;\;\;\;\beta=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\openone&0\\
0&-\openone\\
\end{array}\right)=\sigma_{z}\otimes\openone,

(241)

which satisfy the following relations

\displaystyle{\alpha}_{x}^{2}={\alpha}_{y}^{2}={\alpha}_{z}^{2}=\beta^{2}=\mathbb{I},

(242a)

\displaystyle\{{\alpha}_{j},\beta\}={\alpha}_{j}\beta+\beta{\alpha}_{j}=0,\;\;\;\;(j=x,y,z),

(242b)

with
\openone
being the
2\times 2
identity matrix, and
\mathbb{I}=\openone\otimes\openone
being the
4\times 4
identity matrix.

•

The spin-dependent Coulomb-type interaction is referred to a novel kind of interactions between two charged particles
by considering spin. In the non-relativistic quantum mechanics, the spin-dependent Coulomb-type interaction is described through
the Schrödinger-type Hamiltonian as follows

\mathcal{H}=\frac{\vec{\Pi}^{\,2}}{2M}+V(r)=\frac{\vec{\Pi}^{\,2}}{2M}+q\varphi,

(243)

with the canonical momentum as

\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}.

(244)

Similarly, in the relativistic quantum mechanics, the Coulomb interaction is described through the Dirac-type Hamiltonian as follows

\mathcal{H}=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi.

(245)

For the type-I case, the spin potential
\{\vec{\mathcal{A}},\varphi\}
is taken as Eq. (
237
), and for the type-II case, the spin potential
\{\vec{\mathcal{A}},\varphi\}
is taken as Eq. (
238
).

•

We have denoted
q
and
Q
as the “charge” of the particles. The “charge” can be either the usual electric charge (such as the “electric charge” of an electron and a proton), or other types of charge (such as “magnetic charge”, “color charge” or “flavor charge”). In this work, when we take
q
and
Q
as the usual electric charge, then the spin-dependent Coulomb-type interaction means the the spin-dependent Coulomb interaction.

\blacksquare

V
Solving Schrödinger’s Equation with Spin-Dependent Coulomb Interaction

In this section, we come to solve Schrödinger’s equation with the type-I and type-II spin-dependent Coulomb interactions, respectively. Here
g,k,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}
are treated as free parameters, and
\kappa_{4}=0
for it is trivial.

V.1
The Type-I Case

In this case, one has the parameters
g=0,k=0
. The Schrödinger-type Hamiltonian is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+q\varphi=\frac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(246)

Due to Eq. (
82
), we have the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}}{r^{2}}\right)+\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}

(247)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r},

which contains three parameters
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}
.

V.1.1
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{2}=0

In this situation, the Hamiltonian in Eq. (
247
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r},

(248)

and the eigen-problem reads

\mathcal{H}\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi),

(249)

where
E
is the energy and
\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)
is the wavefunction.

Let us denote the operator

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}=\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}),

(250)

and note that the operators
\vec{\ell}^{\;2}
and
(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})
do not contain
r
, therefore

\displaystyle[\mathcal{H},\,\hat{\Lambda}]=0.

(251)

We shall solve the eigen-problem in the common set
\{\mathcal{H},\hat{\Lambda}\}
.
Let us separate the wavefunction into the radial part and the angular part as follows

\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi),

(252)

which satisfies

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=E\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2}\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi),

(253)

where
E
and
\lambda
are the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{H}
and
\hat{\Lambda}
, respectively. Then we have the radial differential equation as

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\lambda(\lambda+1)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,R(r)=E\,R(r),

(254)

and the angular differential equation as

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2}\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi).

(255)

1.1 Solving the Angular Differential Equation

Firstly, let us come to solve the angular differential equation Eq. (
255
).

Remark 8
.

The Eigen-Problem of
\vec{J}^{\;2}
. Let the “total” angular momentum operator is

\vec{J}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S},

(256)

where
\vec{S}
is the spin-
1/2
angular momentum operator. The common eigenstates of
\{\vec{J}^{\;2},\vec{\ell}^{\;2},J_{z}\}
are given as

\displaystyle\vec{J}^{\;2}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j(j+1)\hbar^{2}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}^{\;2}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l(l+1)\hbar^{2}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle J_{z}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{j}\hbar\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi).

(257)

More precisely, one has

Case A.—
For the quantum number
j=l+1/2
,
(m_{j}=m+1/2)
, the eigenstate is given by

\displaystyle\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}.

(258)

Case B.—
For the quantum number
j=l-1/2
,
l\neq 0
,
(m_{j}=m+1/2)
, the eigenstate
is given by

\displaystyle\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}.

(259)

Since the right-hand side of Eq. (
258
) and Eq. (
259
) appear only quantum numbers
l
and
m
, for simplicity, without any confusion we may denote
\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\equiv\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)
and
\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\equiv\Phi^{B}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)
.
\blacksquare

Remark 9
.

Eigenstates of
(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})
. Let us denote

\displaystyle S_{r}=\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S},\;\;\;\;\;\sigma_{r}=\hat{r}\cdot\vec{\sigma},

(260)

we attain

\displaystyle S_{r}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\sigma_{r}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\begin{bmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
\sin\theta\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}&-\cos\theta\end{bmatrix},

(261)

According to
1991Lange
, some useful recursive formulas about
Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)
can make

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi),

(262)

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)=-\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi).

(263)

Thus the eigenfunction of
{S}_{r}
can be attained

\displaystyle S_{r}\Phi^{s_{r}}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=s_{r}\hbar\Phi^{s_{r}}_{lm}(\theta,\phi),

(264)

where

\displaystyle\Phi^{s_{r}}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=\begin{cases}&\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],\qquad s_{r}=+\dfrac{1}{2},\\
&\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)+\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],\qquad s_{r}=-\dfrac{1}{2}.\end{cases}

(265)

\blacksquare

Now we let

\displaystyle\Theta(\theta,\phi)_{lm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{l}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]+b_{l}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)+\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]

(266)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(a_{l}+b_{l})\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi){-}(a_{l}-b_{l})\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],

then from

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)=\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2}\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi),

(267)

we have

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{(a_{l}+b_{l})l(l+1)\hbar^{2}\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi){-}(a_{l}-b_{l})(l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right\}

(268)

\displaystyle+2Mq\kappa_{1}\left\{a_{l}\,\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]-b_{l}\,\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)+\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(a_{l}+b_{l})l(l+1)\hbar^{2}+(a_{l}-b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\right]\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle+\left[{-}(a_{l}-b_{l})(l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}-(a_{l}+b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\right]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2}\,\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2}\,\left[(a_{l}+b_{l})\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi){-}(a_{l}-b_{l})\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],

then we have

\displaystyle(a_{l}+b_{l})l(l+1)\hbar^{2}+(a_{l}-b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2}=(a_{l}+b_{l})\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2},

\displaystyle{-}(a_{l}-b_{l})(l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}-(a_{l}+b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2}={-}(a_{l}-b_{l})\lambda(\lambda+1)\,\hbar^{2},

(269)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(a_{l}+b_{l})\left[\lambda(\lambda+1)-l(l+1)\right]\hbar^{2}=(a_{l}-b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2},

\displaystyle(a_{l}-b_{l})\left[\lambda(\lambda+1)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]\hbar^{2}=(a_{l}+b_{l})2Mq\kappa_{1}\dfrac{\hbar}{2},

(270)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(a_{l}+b_{l})\left[\lambda(\lambda+1)-l(l+1)\right]=(a_{l}-b_{l})\dfrac{Mq\kappa_{1}}{\hbar},

\displaystyle(a_{l}-b_{l})\left[\lambda(\lambda+1)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]=(a_{l}+b_{l})\dfrac{Mq\kappa_{1}}{\hbar}.

(271)

For simplicity, we denote

\displaystyle\Lambda=\lambda(\lambda+1),

(272)

then we have

\displaystyle\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]=\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}},

(273)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{2}-\left[l(l+1)+(l+1)(l+2)\right]\Lambda+l(l+1)(l+1)(l+2)-\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}=0,

(274)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\pm}=\dfrac{\left[l(l+1)+(l+1)(l+2)\right]\pm\sqrt{\left[l(l+1)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]^{2}+4\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{2},

(275)

i.e,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\pm}=\dfrac{2(l+1)^{2}\pm\sqrt{4(l+1)^{2}+4\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{2},

(276)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\pm}=(l+1)^{2}\pm\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}.

(277)

By considering when
q\kappa_{1}=0
, the operator
\hat{\Lambda}
reduces to
\vec{\ell}^{\,2}
and the corresponding eigenvalue reduces to
l(l+1)\hbar^{2}
, thus we keep
\Lambda_{-}
and abandon
\Lambda_{+}
. Namely,

\displaystyle\Lambda=(l+1)^{2}-\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}(q\kappa_{1})^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}.

(278)

Let

\displaystyle a_{l}=\cos\chi,\;\;\;\;\;b_{l}=\sin\chi,

(279)

from Eq. (
V.1.1
) we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{(a_{l}+b_{l})}{(a_{l}-b_{l})}\dfrac{\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]}{\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}=\dfrac{(a_{l}-b_{l})}{(a_{l}+b_{l})},

(280)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{(a_{l}+b_{l})^{2}}{(a_{l}-b_{l})^{2}}=\dfrac{\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}{\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]},

(281)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1+\sin(2\chi)}{1-\sin(2\chi)}=\dfrac{\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}{\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]},

(282)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[{1+\sin(2\chi)}\right]{\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]}=\left[{1-\sin(2\chi)}\right]{\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]},

(283)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\{\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right]+\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]\right\}\sin(2\chi)=\left[\Lambda-(l+1)(l+2)\right]-\left[\Lambda-l(l+1)\right],

(284)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[2\Lambda-2(l+1)^{2}\right]\sin(2\chi)=-2(l+1),

(285)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\Lambda-(l+1)^{2}\right]\sin(2\chi)=-(l+1),

(286)

thus,

\displaystyle\sin(2\chi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{-(l+1)}{\left[\Lambda-(l+1)^{2}\right]}=\dfrac{-(l+1)}{-\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}|q\kappa_{1}|^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{M^{2}|q\kappa_{1}|^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}}}}.

(287)

So far, we have obtained the eigenfunctions (i.e.,
\Theta_{lm}(\theta,\phi)
) and eigenvalues (i.e.,
\Lambda
) for the angular differential equation. In addition, from Eq. (
272
) we can have

\displaystyle\lambda=\dfrac{-1\pm\sqrt{1+4\Lambda}}{2}.

(288)

1.2 Solving the Radial Differential Equation

Secondly, let us come to solve the angular differential equation as shown in Eq. (
254
). Let us compare Eq. (
254
) and Eq. (
98
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\lambda(\lambda+1)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,R(r)=E\,R(r),

(289)

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}+\dfrac{l(l+1)\hbar^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)+\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right]R(r)=E\,R(r).

(290)

One finds that they are almost the same except the following replacement:

\displaystyle l\rightarrow\lambda,\;\;\;\kappa\rightarrow\kappa_{3}.

(291)

The difference is that
l
is an integer, while
\lambda
is generally not an integer if
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
. Therefore,
for the case of attractive potential (i.e.,
q\kappa_{3}<0
), the eigenfunctions and the energy spectrum are obtained similarly as follows:

\displaystyle R(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{\lambda}{\rm e}^{-\sqrt{-\epsilon}\,r}F\left((\lambda+1)+\dfrac{Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}\sqrt{-\epsilon}};\,2(\lambda+1);\,2\sqrt{-\epsilon}\,r\right),

(292)

\displaystyle\epsilon=\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}<0,

(293)

\displaystyle E=-\dfrac{M(q\kappa_{3})^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{\left(N+\lambda+1\right)^{2}}<0,\;\;(N=0,1,2,3,...),

(294)

where

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{+}=\dfrac{-1+\sqrt{1+4\Lambda}}{2}>0.

(295)

When
\kappa_{1}=0
,
\lambda
becomes
l
, and the above results reduce to those of the usual hydrogen atom.

V.1.2
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{2}\neq 0

In this case, from Eq. (
247
) we have the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}+\boxed{q\kappa_{2}\,r\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}.

(296)

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables. Alternatively, one may re-write the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}+\mathcal{H}_{I},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boxed{q\kappa_{2}\,r\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})},

(297)

where the term
\mathcal{H}_{I}
is treated as an additional interaction based on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

Remark 10
.

In the following, we try to study the eigen-problem further without the perturbation theory. Let us rewrite Eq. (
296
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(298)

Let us treat the operator “
\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})
” as a whole. Namely, we
define the
r
-dependent operator as

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}),

(299)

and we set its
r
-dependent eigenfunctions as

\displaystyle\Theta(r,\theta,\phi)_{lm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{l}(r)\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]+b_{l}(r)\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)+\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]

(300)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],

and its
r
-dependent eigenvalue as
\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)=\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2}\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi),

(301)

here
a_{l}(r)
,
b_{l}(r)
are some coefficients depending on
r
.

We can have

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})\right]\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)

(302)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]l(l+1)\hbar^{2}\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)](l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right\}

\displaystyle+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\left\{a_{l}(r)\,\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]-b_{l}(r)\,\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\left[\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)+\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]l(l+1)\hbar^{2}+[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\right]\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle+\left[-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)](l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}-[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\right]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2}\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2}\,\left[[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],

then we have

\displaystyle[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]l(l+1)\hbar^{2}+[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2}=[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2},

\displaystyle-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)](l+1)(l+2)\hbar^{2}-[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2}=-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2},

(303)

i.e.,

\displaystyle[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]\hbar^{2}=[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2},

\displaystyle[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]\hbar^{2}=[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)\dfrac{\hbar}{2},

(304)

i.e.,

\displaystyle[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]=[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\dfrac{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}{\hbar},

\displaystyle[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]=[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\dfrac{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}{\hbar}.

(305)

Then we have

\displaystyle\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]=\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}},

(306)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)^{2}-\left[l(l+1)+(l+1)(l+2)\right]\Lambda(r)+l(l+1)(l+1)(l+2)-\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}=0,

(307)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)_{\pm}=\dfrac{\left[l(l+1)+(l+1)(l+2)\right]\pm\sqrt{\left[l(l+1)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]^{2}+4\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{2},

(308)

i.e,

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)_{\pm}=\dfrac{2(l+1)^{2}\pm\sqrt{4(l+1)^{2}+4\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{2},

(309)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)_{\pm}=(l+1)^{2}\pm\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}.

(310)

As one expects, when
\kappa_{2}=0
,
\Lambda(r)_{\pm}
reduces to
\Lambda_{\pm}
as shown in Eq. (
277
), which does not depend on
r
.

By considering when
q\kappa_{1}=q\kappa_{2}=0
, the operator
\hat{\Lambda}
reduces to
\vec{\ell}^{\,2}
and the corresponding eigenvalue reduces to
l(l+1)\hbar^{2}
, thus we keep
\Lambda_{-}
and abandon
\Lambda_{+}
. Namely,

\displaystyle\Lambda(r)=(l+1)^{2}-\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}.

(311)

Similarly, let

\displaystyle a_{l}(r)=\cos\chi,\;\;\;\;\;b_{l}(r)=\sin\chi,

(312)

we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]}{[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]}\dfrac{\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]}{\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}=\dfrac{[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]}{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]},

(313)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]^{2}}{[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]^{2}}=\dfrac{\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}{\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]},

(314)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1+\sin(2\chi)}{1-\sin(2\chi)}=\dfrac{\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]}{\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]},

(315)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[{1+\sin(2\chi)}\right]{\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]}=\left[{1-\sin(2\chi)}\right]{\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]},

(316)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\{\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right]+\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]\right\}\sin(2\chi)=\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)(l+2)\right]-\left[\Lambda(r)-l(l+1)\right],

(317)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[2\Lambda(r)-2(l+1)^{2}\right]\sin(2\chi)=-2(l+1),

(318)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)^{2}\right]\sin(2\chi)=-(l+1),

(319)

thus,

\displaystyle\sin(2\chi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{-(l+1)}{\left[\Lambda(r)-(l+1)^{2}\right]}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}}}=\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}},

(320)

and

\displaystyle\cos(2\chi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}.

(321)

The coefficients
a_{l}(r)
and
b_{l}(r)
can be calculated through

\displaystyle a_{l}(r)=\cos\chi=\sqrt{\dfrac{1+\cos(2\chi)}{2}},\;\;\;\;\;b_{l}(r)=\sin\chi=\sqrt{\dfrac{1-\cos(2\chi)}{2}}.

(322)

The eigen-problem is given by

\mathcal{H}\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi),

(323)

where
E
is the energy and
\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)
is the wavefunction.
We now recast the wavefunction
\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)
as

\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi),

(324)

note that in this case, due to
\kappa_{2}\neq 0
,
\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)
has not been totally separated between
r
-space and
(\theta,\phi)
-space.
However, the following relation is still valid, i.e.,

\displaystyle\hat{\Lambda}(r)\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=\hat{\Lambda}(r)\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)=\Lambda(r)\,\hbar^{2}\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi).

(325)

The eigen-problem becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi),

(326)

After substituting Eq. (
325
) into Eq. (
326
), we have

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi),

(327)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,R(r)\,\Theta_{lm}(r,\theta,\phi),

(328)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,R(r)\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right\}

(329)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\Phi^{A}_{lm}(\theta,\phi)-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\Phi^{B}_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\right\},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\times

\displaystyle R(r)\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\,\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\,\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\times

\displaystyle R(r)\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\,\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\,\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]R(r)\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\sqrt{l+m+1}+[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\sqrt{l-m}\right\}

(332a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\sqrt{l+m+1}+[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\sqrt{l-m}\right\},

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]R(r)\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\sqrt{l-m}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\sqrt{l+m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,\left\{[a_{l}(r)+b_{l}(r)]\,\sqrt{l-m}-[a_{l}(r)-b_{l}(r)]\sqrt{l+m+1}\right\},

(332b)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]

(333a)

\displaystyle R(r)\left\{a_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]+b_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}-\sqrt{l-m}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{a_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]+b_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}-\sqrt{l-m}\right]\right\},

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]

\displaystyle R(r)\left\{a_{l}(r)\left[-\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]+b_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\,R(r)\,\left\{a_{l}(r)\left[-\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]+b_{l}(r)\left[\sqrt{l+m+1}+\sqrt{l-m}\right]\right\}.

(333b)

Then we have

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\}=E\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\},

(334a)

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,\left\{R(r)b_{l}(r)\right\}=E\,\left\{R(r)b_{l}(r)\right\},

(334b)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\}=E\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\},

(335a)

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)\hbar^{2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}\right]\,\left\{R(r)b_{l}(r)\right\}=E\,\left\{R(r)b_{l}(r)\right\}.

(335b)

If
a_{l}(r)
and
b_{l}(r)
do not depend on
r
, then
\Lambda(r)
does not depend on
r
, the above two equations are the same and naturally reduce to Eq. (
254
).

Because

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}=\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}a_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r},

(336)

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[R(r)a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}=\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}a_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\right]=\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}a_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+2\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r},

then we have

\displaystyle\left[\left(\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\,r}\right)-\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}-\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}\right]\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\}=-\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\},

(338)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}a_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+2\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\right]+\dfrac{2}{r}\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}a_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]\,\left\{R(r)a_{l}(r)\right\}=0.

(339)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}b_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+2\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\right]+\dfrac{2}{r}\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}b_{l}(r)+R(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]\,\left\{R(r)b_{l}(r)\right\}=0.

(340)

Because the following normalized condition

\displaystyle a^{2}_{l}(r)+b^{2}_{l}(r)=\cos^{2}\chi+\sin^{2}\chi=1,

(341)

thus

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}[a^{2}_{l}(r)+b^{2}_{l}(r)]}{{\rm d}r}=a_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}a_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}+b_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}b_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}=0.

(342)

Then the sum of
a_{l}(r)
multiplying Eq. (
10
) and
b_{l}(r)
multiplying Eq. (
10
) gives

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+R(r)\left(a_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+b_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}\right)\right]+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[R(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}-\left[\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]\,\left\{R(r)\right\}=0.

Because

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}\left(a_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}a_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}+b_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}b_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}\right)=\left(a_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+b_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}\right)+\left(\dfrac{{\rm d}a_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}\right)^{2}+\left(\dfrac{{\rm d}b_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}\right)^{2}=0,

(344)

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\cos\chi}{{\rm d}r}=\dfrac{{\rm d}\cos\chi}{{\rm d}\chi}\dfrac{{\rm d}\chi}{{\rm d}r}=-\sin\chi\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}\left[\dfrac{1}{2}\arcsin\left(\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\right)\right]

(345)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sin\chi\,\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\left(\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\right)^{2}}}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}\left[\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sin\chi\,\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}\left[\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sin\chi\,\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}\left[\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar\,(-\dfrac{1}{2})2M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}\right]^{3/2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\chi\,\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\dfrac{Mq\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}{\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\dfrac{3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b_{l}(r)\dfrac{1}{2}\sin(2\chi)\cos(2\chi)\dfrac{3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}.

Similarly, one has

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}b_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}=-\dfrac{a_{l}(r)}{b_{l}(r)}\dfrac{{\rm d}a_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}=-\dfrac{a_{l}(r)}{b_{l}(r)}b_{l}(r)\dfrac{1}{2}\sin(2\chi)\cos(2\chi)\dfrac{3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}=-a_{l}(r)\dfrac{1}{2}\sin(2\chi)\cos(2\chi)\dfrac{3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}.

(346)

thus

\displaystyle\left(a_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[a_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+b_{l}(r)\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\left[b_{l}(r)\right]}{{\rm d}r^{2}}\right)=-\left(\dfrac{{\rm d}a_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}\right)^{2}-\left(\dfrac{{\rm d}b_{l}(r)}{{\rm d}r}\right)^{2}=-\left(\dfrac{1}{2}\sin(2\chi)\cos(2\chi)\dfrac{3\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\dfrac{3}{2}\dfrac{(l+1)\hbar Mq\kappa_{2}r^{2}}{{(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}}}\right)^{2}=-\dfrac{9}{4}\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}M^{2}q^{2}\kappa^{2}_{2}r^{4}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}}.

From Eq. (
10
) we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}R(r)}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+R(r)\left(-\dfrac{9}{4}\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}M^{2}q^{2}\kappa^{2}_{2}r^{4}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}}\right)+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}R(r)}{{\rm d}r}-\left[\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]\,R(r)=0.

i.e,

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}R(r)}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}R(r)}{{\rm d}r}-\left[\dfrac{9}{4}\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}M^{2}q^{2}\kappa^{2}_{2}r^{4}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}}+\dfrac{\Lambda(r)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]\,R(r)=0,

(349)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}R(r)}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}R(r)}{{\rm d}r}-\Biggr[\dfrac{9}{4}\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}M^{2}q^{2}\kappa^{2}_{2}r^{4}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}-\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\left(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2}r^{3}\right)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}{-}\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\Biggr]\,R(r)=0,

(350)

which reduces to Eq. (
254
) when
\kappa_{2}=0
. Eq. (
10
) is the differential equation that
R(r)
satisfies, however, it seems that it cannot be exactly solved.
\blacksquare

V.1.3
\kappa_{1}=0
and
\kappa_{2}\neq 0

In this case, based on Eq. (
296
), we can have a simplified Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}\right)+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}}{2Mr^{2}}+\frac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}+\boxed{q\kappa_{2}\,r\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}.

(351)

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables. We will investigate its energy spectrum by the perturbation theory method in the future work. Based on Eq. (
10
), let
\kappa_{1}=0
, we have the differential equation for
R(r)
as

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}R(r)}{{\rm d}r^{2}}+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}R(r)}{{\rm d}r}-\left[\dfrac{9}{4}\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}\hbar^{2}M^{2}q^{2}\kappa^{2}_{2}r^{4}}{\left[(l+1)^{2}\,\hbar^{2}+{M^{2}q^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}r^{6}}\right]^{2}}+\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}-\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}+\dfrac{M^{2}q^{2}\kappa_{2}^{2}r^{6}}{\hbar^{2}}}}{r^{2}}+\frac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}+\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}\right]R(r)=0.

V.2
The Type-II Case

In this case, one has the parameters
g\neq 0,k\neq 0
, and
g\hbar k=1
. The Schrödinger-type Hamiltonian is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\vec{\Pi}^{2}}{2M}+q\varphi=\dfrac{\left(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)^{2}}{2M}+q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r},

(353)

with

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}.

(354)

For simplicity, we consider merely the spin-
1/2
case, i.e.,
\vec{S}=\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\vec{\sigma}
.

Because

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\cdot\left(k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}\right)

(355)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{x}}{r^{2}}+k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{y}}{r^{2}}+k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial z}\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\dfrac{\left(yS_{z}-zS_{y}\right)}{r^{2}}+k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\dfrac{\left(zS_{x}-xS_{z}\right)}{r^{2}}+k\dfrac{\partial}{\partial z}\dfrac{\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\left(yS_{z}-zS_{y}\right)\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}+k\left(zS_{x}-xS_{z}\right)\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}+k\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)\dfrac{\partial}{\partial z}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\left(yS_{z}-zS_{y}\right)\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right]\dfrac{\partial r}{\partial x}+k\left(zS_{x}-xS_{z}\right)\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right]\dfrac{\partial r}{\partial y}+k\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)\left[\dfrac{\partial}{\partial r}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right]\dfrac{\partial r}{\partial z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k\left(yS_{z}-zS_{y}\right)\dfrac{-2}{r^{3}}\dfrac{x}{r}+k\left(zS_{x}-xS_{z}\right)\dfrac{-2}{r^{3}}\dfrac{y}{r}+k\left(xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right)\dfrac{-2}{r^{3}}\dfrac{z}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{-2k}{r^{4}}\left[\vec{r}\cdot\left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)\right]=0,

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{k\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{(\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma})\cdot\vec{p}}{r^{2}}=-\dfrac{g\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{(\vec{\sigma}\times\vec{r})\cdot\vec{p}}{r^{2}}=-\dfrac{k\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{\vec{\sigma}\cdot(\vec{r}\times\vec{p})}{r^{2}}=-\dfrac{k\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{\sigma}}{r^{2}}=-k\dfrac{\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{2}},

(356)

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}^{\,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{k\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma}}{r^{2}}\right)\cdot\left(\dfrac{k\,\hbar}{2}\dfrac{\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma}}{r^{2}}\right)=\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{4\,r^{4}}(\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma})\cdot(\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma})=\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{4\,r^{4}}\bigl[(\vec{r}\times\vec{\sigma})\times\vec{r}\bigr]\cdot\vec{\sigma}

(357)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{4\,r^{4}}\bigl[(r^{2}\vec{\sigma})-(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{r})\vec{r}\bigr]\cdot\vec{\sigma}=\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{4\,r^{4}}\bigl(3r^{2}-r^{2}\bigr)=\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}},

we then have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\vec{\Pi}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{1}{2M}\left(\vec{p}^{\,2}-\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{p}-\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{A}}+\vec{\mathcal{A}}^{\,2}\right)

(358)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{1}{2M}\biggl[\vec{p}^{\,2}-\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{p}-\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{p}-{\rm i}\hbar\vec{\nabla}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)+\vec{\mathcal{A}}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{1}{2M}\left(\vec{p}^{\,2}-2\vec{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\vec{p}+\vec{\mathcal{A}}^{\,2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{1}{2M}\left(\vec{p}^{\,2}+2k\,\dfrac{\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right).

Then the Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(\vec{p}^{\,2}+2k\,\dfrac{\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right)+q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(359)

Due to Eq. (
82
), we have the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\dfrac{\vec{\ell}^{\;2}}{r^{2}}+2k\,\dfrac{\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{2}}+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right)+q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}

(360)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left\{-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\dfrac{\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2k\left(\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\right]}{r^{2}}\right\}+q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

V.2.1
\kappa_{1}=0
and
\kappa_{3}\neq 0

In this case, the Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left\{-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\dfrac{\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2k\left(\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\right]}{r^{2}}\right\}+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(361)

Remark 11
.

The Eigen-Problem of
\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}
.
Since

\displaystyle\left[\vec{J}^{\,2},\ \vec{\ell}^{\,2}\right]=0,\;\;\;\left[\vec{J}^{\,2},\ \vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right]=0,\;\;\;\left[\vec{\ell}^{\,2},\ \vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right]=0,

(362)

thus three operators
\{\vec{J}^{\,2},\vec{\ell}^{\,2},\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\}
share the same eigenstates. From

\displaystyle\vec{J}^{\,2}=\left(\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}\right)^{2},

(363)

one can have

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\vec{J}^{\,2}-\vec{\ell}^{\;2}-\dfrac{3}{4}\hbar^{2}\right).

(364)

We then have the eigenvalues and eigenstates for the operator
\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}
as follows:

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\hbar^{2}}{2}\left[j(j+1)-l(l+1)-\dfrac{3}{4}\right]\,\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

(365)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}&\dfrac{l}{2}\,\hbar^{2}\;\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;{\rm for}\;\;j=l+\dfrac{1}{2},\\
&-\dfrac{l+1}{2}\,\hbar^{2}\;\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),\;\;\;{\rm for}\;\;j=l-\dfrac{1}{2},\;(l\neq 0),\end{cases}

with
m_{j}=m+1/2
.
\blacksquare

We now separate the wavefunction as

\displaystyle\Psi(\vec{r})=\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=R(r)\;\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

(366)

where the two-component quantum state
\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
,
or
\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
, as we have shown in Eqs. (
258
) and (
259
).
We then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}^{\;2}\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l(l+1)\hbar^{2}\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle W\hbar^{2}\;R(r)\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

(367)

where
W=l/2
or
-(l+1)/2
(
l\neq 0
), depending on
\Phi_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
takes

\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
or
\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
. By substituting the last two
equations of (
V.2.1
) into the first equation of (
V.2.1
), we then have the following
radial eigen-equation:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left\{-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\left[l(l+1)\hbar^{2}+2kW\hbar^{2}+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\right]\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right\}R(r)+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}R(r)=E\,R(r),

(368)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hbar^{2}}{2M}\left\{-\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\left[l(l+1)+2kW+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}\right]\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right\}R(r)+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}R(r)=E\,R(r),

(369)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hbar^{2}}{2M}\left\{-\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}-2\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\,r}+\left[l(l+1)+2kW+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}\right]\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right\}R(r)+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}R(r)=E\,R(r).

(370)

Denote

\displaystyle\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{2M}{\hbar^{2}}E,\;\;\;\;\;\;\Omega=l(l+1)+2kW+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2},

(371)

then Eq. (
370
) becomes

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}\,R(r)}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}+\dfrac{2}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}\,R(r)}{{\rm d}\,r}-\dfrac{\Omega}{r^{2}}R(r)-\dfrac{2Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}r}R(r)+\epsilon R(r)=0.

(372)

Observation 1.—
The parameter
\Omega
in Eq. (
371
) is non-negative.

Proof.

(i)

For
W=\dfrac{l}{2}
, because
k\neq 0
and
l\geq 0
, we then have

\Omega=l(l+1)+2kW+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}=l(l+1)+kl+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}=\left(\dfrac{k}{2}+l\right)^{2}+\dfrac{k^{2}}{4}+l>0.

(373)

(ii)

For
W=-\dfrac{l+1}{2}
, (
l\neq 0
), because
l\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}\Omega=&\ l(l+1)+2kW+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}=l(l+1)-k(l+1)+\dfrac{k^{2}}{2}\\
=&\ \dfrac{1}{2}\Bigl[k^{2}-2k(l+1)+2l(l+1)\Bigr]=\dfrac{1}{2}\Bigl\{\bigl[k-(l+1)\bigr]^{2}+2l(l+1)-(l+1)^{2}\Bigr\}\\
=&\ \dfrac{1}{2}\Bigl\{\bigl[k-(l+1)\bigr]^{2}+(l+1)(l-1)\Bigr\}\\
=&\ \dfrac{1}{2}\Bigl\{\bigl[k-(l+1)\bigr]^{2}+l^{2}-1\Bigr\}\geq 0,\end{split}

(374)

This ends the proof.
∎

Let us denote

\displaystyle\Omega=\lambda(\lambda+1),

(375)

then Eq. (
370
) becomes

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hbar^{2}}{2M}\left\{-\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}\,r^{2}}-2\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\,r}+\dfrac{\lambda(\lambda+1)}{r^{2}}\right\}R(r)+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}R(r)=E\,R(r).

(376)

Let us compare Eq. (
376
) and Eq. (
98
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\dfrac{1}{2M}\left(-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{{\rm d}^{2}}{{\rm d}r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}r}+\dfrac{l(l+1)\hbar^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)+\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right]R(r)=E\,R(r).

(377)

One finds that they are almost the same except the following replacement:

\displaystyle l\rightarrow\lambda,\;\;\;\kappa\rightarrow\kappa_{3}.

(378)

The difference is that
l
is an integer, while
\lambda
is generally not an integer because
k\neq 0
. Therefore, for the case of attractive potential, i.e.,
q\kappa_{3}<0
, the eigenfunctions and the energy spectrum are as follows:

\displaystyle R(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{\lambda}{\rm e}^{-\sqrt{-\epsilon}\,r}F\left((\lambda+1)+\dfrac{Mq\kappa_{3}}{\hbar^{2}\sqrt{-\epsilon}};\,2(\lambda+1);\,2\sqrt{-\epsilon}\,r\right),

(379)

\displaystyle\epsilon=\dfrac{2ME}{\hbar^{2}}<0,

(380)

\displaystyle E=-\dfrac{M(q\kappa_{3})^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{\left(N+\lambda+1\right)^{2}}<0,\;\;(N=0,1,2,3,...).

(381)

where

\displaystyle\lambda=\lambda_{+}=\dfrac{-1+\sqrt{1+4\Omega}}{2}>0.

(382)

If
k=0
, then
\lambda
becomes
l
, and the above results reduce to those of the usual hydrogen atom.

V.2.2
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{3}\neq 0

In this case, the Hamiltonian reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left\{-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2k\left(\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\right]\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right\}+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}+\boxed{q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})}.

(383)

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables.
Alternatively, one may re-write the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}+\mathcal{H}_{I},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2M}\left\{-\hbar^{2}\dfrac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\,r^{2}}-2\hbar^{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\dfrac{\partial}{\partial\,r}+\left[\vec{\ell}^{\;2}+2k\left(\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{k^{2}\,\hbar^{2}}{2}\right]\dfrac{1}{r^{2}}\right\}+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boxed{q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})},

(384)

where the term
\mathcal{H}_{I}
is treated as an additional interaction based on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

VI
Solving Dirac’s Equation with Spin-Dependent Coulomb Interaction

In this section, we come to solve Dirac’s equation with the type-I and type-II spin-dependent Coulomb interactions, respectively.
Here
g,k,\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}
are treated as free parameters, and we set
\kappa_{4}=0
for it is trivial.

VI.1
The Type-I Case

In this case, one has the parameters
g=0,k=0
. The Dirac-type Hamiltonian is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta Mc^{2}+\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(385)
