which contains three parameters
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}
.

Because the Dirac-type Hamilton is related to the spin operators of two particles, in order to distinguish them clearly, we now introduce the subscripts. Since the Dirac matrices
\{\vec{\alpha},\beta\}
correspond to particle 1, thus we rewrite them as

\{\vec{\alpha}_{1},\beta_{1}\}
. Explicitly, they are

\vec{\alpha}_{1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\vec{\sigma}_{1}\\
\vec{\sigma}_{1}&0\\
\end{array}\right),\;\;\;\;\;\beta_{1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\openone_{1}&0\\
0&-\openone_{1}\\
\end{array}\right),

(386)

where
\vec{\sigma}_{1}=(\sigma_{1x},\sigma_{1y},\sigma_{1z})
is the Pauli-matrices vector for particle 1, and

\openone_{1}
is the
2\times 2
identity matrix of particle 1. Similarly, the original spin operator

\vec{S}=\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\vec{\sigma}
corresponds to particle 2, thus we rewrite it as

\displaystyle\vec{S}_{2}=\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\vec{\sigma}_{2}.

(387)

Then the above Dirac-type Hamiltonian turns to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}_{1}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})+\dfrac{q\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(388)

VI.1.1
\kappa_{1}=0
and
\kappa_{2}=0

In this case, the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
is just that of the relativistic hydrogen atom (or hydrogen-like atom), i.e., Eq. (
388
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta Mc^{2}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r}.

(389)

Here, for simplicity, we have rewritten
\vec{\alpha}_{1}
as
\vec{\alpha}
(because there are no the terms related to

\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}
), and we have denoted
\kappa_{3}
as
\kappa
(
\kappa=Q=Ze
,
Z
is an integer,
Z=1
for the hydrogen atom
and
Z\geq 2
for the hydrogen-like atom). The eigen-problem of the relativistic hydrogen-like atom can be exactly solved, which can
be found in textbook of quantum mechanics, such as
1999Flugge
.

1.1. The Discussion About
\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{p}

First, we consider
\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{p}

\displaystyle\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{p}=-{\rm i}\hbar\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{z}&\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y}\\
\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y}&-\partial_{z}\end{pmatrix},

(390)

where
\partial_{x}=\dfrac{\partial}{\partial x}
,
\partial_{y}=\dfrac{\partial}{\partial y}
,
\partial_{z}=\dfrac{\partial}{\partial z}
.

For
\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{z}
, we can represent them with
\partial_{r},\partial_{\theta},\partial_{\phi}
,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}=\sin\theta\cos\phi\,\partial_{r}+\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}-\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi},

(391a)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}=\sin\theta\sin\phi\,\partial_{r}+\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}+\frac{\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi},

(391b)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}=\cos\theta\,\partial_{r}-\frac{\sin\theta}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}.

(391c)

According to some formulas about
Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)
, we can attain

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y})\,[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l+m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m-1}

(392a)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m-1},

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y})\,[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l-m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m+1}

(392b)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+2)(l+m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m+1},

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\,[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l-m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m}

(392c)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m}.

Then for
\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}
and
\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}
, we can attain

\displaystyle(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{p})\,[F(r)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)]={\rm i}\hbar\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

(393a)

\displaystyle(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{p})\,[F(r)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)]={\rm i}\hbar\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}F(r)\right)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi).

(393b)

It means that based on Eq. (
392a
), Eq. (
392b
), and Eq. (
392c
), one can eventually have

\displaystyle(\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{p})\begin{pmatrix}F(r)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
G(r)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}={\rm i}\hbar\begin{pmatrix}\left(\frac{\partial G(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}G(r)\right)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}.

(394)

Proof.

Here, we would like to prove Eq. (
392a
), Eq. (
392b
), and Eq. (
392c
). In the beginning, let us prepare some formulas needed for the proof.

There are some useful recursive formulas about
P_{l}(x)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle(2l+1)xP_{l}(x)=(l+1)P_{l+1}(x)+lP_{l-1}(x),

(395a)

\displaystyle P_{l}(x)=P_{l+1}^{\prime}(x)-2xP_{l}^{\prime}(x)+P_{l-1}^{\prime}(x),

(395b)

\displaystyle P^{\prime}_{l+1}(x)=xP_{l}^{\prime}(x)+(l+1)P_{l}(x),

(395c)

\displaystyle P_{l-1}^{\prime}(x)=xP^{\prime}_{l}(x)-lP_{l}(x),

(395d)

where
P_{l}^{\prime}(x)=\dfrac{d}{dx}P_{l}(x)
. According to Eq. (
395c
) and Eq. (
395d
), we have

\displaystyle(1-x^{2})P_{l+1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{l+1}^{\prime}-x(xP_{l+1}^{\prime})=\left[xP_{l}^{\prime}+(l+1)P_{l}\right]-x\left[P_{l}^{\prime}+(l+1)P_{l+1}\right]

(396)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(l+1)P_{l}-(l+1)xP_{l+1}=(l+1)[P_{l}-xP_{l+1}],

or

\displaystyle(1-x^{2})P_{l}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l[P_{l-1}-xP_{l}].

(397)

In addition,

\displaystyle[(1-x^{2})P_{l}^{\prime}]^{(|m|)}=(1-x^{2})P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}-2|m|xP_{l}^{(|m|)}-(|m|-1)|m|P_{l}^{(|m|-1)},

(398)

then

\displaystyle(1-x^{2})P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}=l\left[P_{l-1}^{(|m|)}-(xP_{l})^{(|m|)}\right]+2|m|xP_{l}^{(|m|)}+(|m|-1)|m|P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}.

(399)

Based on Eq. (
395d
), we can have

\displaystyle P_{l-1}^{(|m|)}-(xP_{l})^{(|m|)}=[P_{l-1}^{\prime}-xP_{l}^{\prime}-P_{l}]^{(|m|-1)}=[-lP_{l}-P_{l}]^{(|m|-1)}=-(l+1)P_{l}^{(|m|-1)},

(400)

and

\displaystyle|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}

(401)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\left[lP_{l-1}^{(|m|)}-l(\cos\theta P_{l})^{(|m|)}+2|m|\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}+(|m|-1)|m|P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{|m|(-\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}+{\cos\theta[(l+1)l-(|m|-1)|m|][P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}]}\right\}.

Similarly, based on Eq. (
395a
) and Eq. (
395b
), we have

\displaystyle(2l+1)P_{l}=P_{l+1}^{\prime}-P_{l-1}^{\prime}.

(402)

(i) Firstly, we consider the case of
m>0
.

Proof of Eq. (
392a
).—
For
\,\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y}=\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}+\cos\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}.

(403)

One can have

\displaystyle\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial(F(r)Y_{lm})}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}Y_{lm}

(404)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}\left\{-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m-1)(l+m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1}+\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}\right\}.

Because

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\left\{(\sin\theta)^{|m|}\frac{d^{|m|}}{d(\cos\theta)^{|m|}}P_{l}(\cos\theta)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\theta\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}-1}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

(405)

\displaystyle=\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\},

where
P_{l}^{(|m|)}=\dfrac{\partial^{|m|}}{\partial(\cos\theta)^{|m|}}P_{l}(\cos\theta)
,
then we have

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{r\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\frac{{\rm i}\partial Y_{lm}}{r\sin\theta\partial\phi}

(406)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}}(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}.

Based on Eq. (
401
), we have

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(407)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(-\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}+\cos\theta[(l+1)l-(|m|-1)|m|][P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}Y_{lm}-\cos\theta[(l+1)l-(|m|-1)|m|]\sqrt{\frac{1}{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)}}Y_{l,m-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}Y_{lm}-\cos\theta\sqrt{{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)}}Y_{l,m-1},

which means

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}=-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l+m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\frac{l+1}{r}Y_{l-1,m-1}-\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\frac{l}{r}Y_{l+1,m-1}.

Then we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y})[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l+m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m-1}

(409)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m-1}.

Thus Eq. (
392a
) is proved.

Proof of Eq. (
392b
).—
For
\,\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y}=\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial}{\partial r}+\cos\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}+e^{\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\phi}.

(410)

One can have

\displaystyle\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial\left[F(r)Y_{lm}\right]}{\partial r}=\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}Y_{lm}

(411)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(l-m-1)(l-m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m+1}-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m+1}\right\},

and

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}+e^{\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(412)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}}(\cos^{2}\theta-1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}})\left\{-|m|P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}.

So based on Eq. (
402
), we have

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}+e^{\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(413)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m+1}+{\sqrt{(l-|m|)(l+|m|+1)}}\cos\theta Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m+1}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{(l-m)(l+m+1)}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+2)(l-|m|)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l+|m|+1)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m+1},

then

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y})[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l-m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m+1}

(414)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+2)(l+m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m+1}.

Thus Eq. (
392b
) is proved.

Proof of Eq. (
392c
).—
For
\partial_{z}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}=\cos\theta\,\frac{\partial}{\partial r}-\sin\theta\frac{1}{r}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}.

(415)

One can have

\displaystyle\cos\theta\frac{\partial[F(r)Y_{lm}]}{\partial r}=\cos\theta\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}Y_{lm}=\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}\left[\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l+m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m}+\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m}\right],

(416)

and

\displaystyle{\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}

(417)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}\sin^{2}\theta\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}-1}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}+1}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}\left\{|m|\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}+{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}-2|m|\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}{\left\{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}-|m|\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}{\left\{\frac{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)}{2l+1}(P_{l+1}^{(|m|)}-P_{l-1}^{(|m|)})-|m|\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)\left\{\sqrt{\frac{l+|m|+1}{(l-|m|+1)(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m}-\sqrt{\frac{l-|m|}{(l+|m|)(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m}\right\}-|m|\cos\theta Y_{lm}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{l\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l-|m|+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m}-(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|)(l+|m|)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m}}.

Based on above, we can attain

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\,[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l-m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m}

(418)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m}.

Thus Eq. (
392c
) is proved.

(ii) Secondly, we consider the case of
m<0
.

For
\,\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y}
, based on Eq. (
405
), one has

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(419)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}}(\cos^{2}\theta-1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}})\left\{-|m|P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m-1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m-1|}{2}})\left\{-|m|P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}.

Similar with Eq. (
413
), one obtains

\displaystyle{\cos\theta e^{{-\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}}

(420)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}+|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1}

\displaystyle{-\sqrt{(l-|m|)(l+|m|+1)}}\cos\theta Y_{l,m-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}+|m|\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1}

\displaystyle{-\sqrt{(l-|m|)(l+|m|+1)}}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+2)(l-|m|)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}+\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l+|m|+1)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-l\sqrt{\frac{(l+|m|+1)(l+|m|+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}-(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l-|m|-1)(l-|m|)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\cos\theta e^{{-\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}-e^{-\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}}

(421)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-l\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+3)(2l+1)}}Y_{l+1,m-1}-(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l+m-1)(l+m)}{(2l-1)(2l+1)}}Y_{l-1,m-1}.

So we can still attain

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}-{\rm i}\partial_{y})[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l+m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m-1}

(422)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m-1}.

For
\,\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y}
, we can attain

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}+e^{\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(423)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}}(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|+1}{2}}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m+1|}{2}})\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}.

Similar with Eq. (
407
), we have

\displaystyle\cos\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\dfrac{1}{r}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}+e^{\rm i\phi}\dfrac{{\rm i}}{r\sin\theta}\frac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\phi}

(424)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}-\cos\theta\sin^{2}\theta P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m+1)\phi}((\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|-1}{2}})\left\{|m|(-\cos^{2}\theta+1)P_{l}^{(|m|)}+\cos\theta[(l+1)l-(|m|-1)|m|][P_{l}^{(|m|-1)}]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\theta}Y_{lm}+\cos\theta[(l+1)l-(|m|-1)|m|]\sqrt{\frac{1}{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)}}Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|m|\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\theta}Y_{lm}+\cos\theta\sqrt{{(l+|m|)(l-|m|+1)}}Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l-m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}({l+1})Y_{l-1,m+1}+\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}{l}Y_{l+1,m+1}.

So we can still attain

\displaystyle(\partial_{x}+{\rm i}\partial_{y})[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l-m-1)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m+1}

(425)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+2)(l+m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m+1}.

For
\partial_{z}
, when
m>0
, one has

\displaystyle{\sin\theta}\dfrac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{m}\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}\sin^{2}\theta\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}-1}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

(426)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m}-(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l+m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m}.

When
m<0
,

\displaystyle{\sin\theta}\dfrac{\partial Y_{lm}}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2l+1}{4\pi}\frac{(l-|m|)!}{(l+|m|)!}}e^{{\rm i}(m)\phi}\sin^{2}\theta\left\{|m|(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}-1}\cos\theta P_{l}^{(|m|)}-(\sin^{2}\theta)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}P_{l}^{(|m|+1)}\right\}

(427)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle l\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}Y_{l+1,m}-(l+1)\sqrt{\frac{(l-m)(l+m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}Y_{l-1,m}.

So we can easily attain

\displaystyle\partial_{z}[F(r)Y_{lm}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(l+m)(l-m)}{(2l+1)(2l-1)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+1}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l-1,m}

(428)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2l+1)(2l+3)}}\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)Y_{l+1,m}.

This ends the proof of Eq. (
392a
), Eq. (
392b
), and Eq. (
392c
).
∎

1.2. The Discussion of Eigen-Problem

The eigen-problem is given by

\mathcal{H}\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,\Psi(r,\theta,\phi),

(429)

where
\mathcal{H}
is the Dirac’s Hamiltonian of the hydrogen-like atom given in Eq. (
389
),
E
is the energy and the form of the wavefunction is as follows

\displaystyle\Psi(r,\theta,\phi)=\begin{pmatrix}F(r)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
G(r)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}.

(430)

Due to Eq. (
394
), we can easily attain

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\begin{pmatrix}\left(\frac{\partial G(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}G(r)\right)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
\left(\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\left(Mc^{2}+\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right)F(r)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
\left(-Mc^{2}+\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right)G(r)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix}=E\begin{pmatrix}F(r)\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\\
G(r)\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)\end{pmatrix},

(431)

i.e.,

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\left\{\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right\}-\left(E+Mc^{2}-\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right)G(r)=0,

(432a)

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\left\{\frac{\partial G(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}G(r)\right\}-\left(E-Mc^{2}-\frac{q\kappa}{r}\right)F(r)=0.

(432b)

When
r\rightarrow\infty
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-(E+Mc^{2})G(r)=0,

(433a)

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\frac{\partial G(r)}{\partial r}-(E-Mc^{2})F(r)=0.

(433b)

If we designate

\displaystyle F=C_{1}e^{-\varepsilon r},\;\;\;\;G=C_{2}e^{-\varepsilon r}

(434)

then we have

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar c\,\varepsilon C_{1}-(E+Mc^{2})C_{2}=0,

(435a)

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar c\,\varepsilon C_{2}-(E-Mc^{2})C_{1}=0,

(435b)

which lead to

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\frac{1}{\hbar c}\sqrt{M^{2}c^{4}-E^{2}},

\displaystyle C_{1}={\rm i}{\sqrt{\frac{Mc^{2}+E}{Mc^{2}-E}}}C_{2}={\rm i}\mu C_{2}.

(436)

Here for simplicity we have denoted

\displaystyle\mu=\sqrt{\frac{Mc^{2}+E}{Mc^{2}-E}}.

(437)

When
r\rightarrow 0
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\left\{\frac{\partial F(r)}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}F(r)\right\}+\frac{q\kappa}{r}G(r)=0,

(438a)

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c\left\{\frac{\partial G(r)}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}G(r)\right\}+\frac{q\kappa}{r}F(r)=0.

(438b)

If we designate

\displaystyle F=C_{1}^{\prime}r^{\nu-1},\;\;\;\ G=C_{2}^{\prime}r^{\nu-1}

(439)

then we have

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c(\nu-1-l)C_{1}^{\prime}+q\kappa C_{2}^{\prime}=0,

(440a)

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar c(\nu+l+1)C_{2}^{\prime}+q\kappa C_{1}^{\prime}=0,

(440b)

which lead to

\displaystyle\nu=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}}=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\tau^{2}},

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\prime}={\rm i}\sqrt{\dfrac{l+1+\nu}{l+1-\nu}}\,C_{2}^{\prime},

(441)

where for simplicity we have denoted

\displaystyle\tau=\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}.

(442)

Note that for the attractive potential, one has
\tau<0
.

Then we designate

\displaystyle F(r)={\rm i}\mu A(r)r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r},\;\;\;\;\;G(r)=B(r)r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r},

(443)

then we have

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\partial A(r)}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu-l-1}{r}-\varepsilon\right)A(r)\right\}+\left(\varepsilon-\frac{\tau}{\mu r}\right)B(r)=0,

(444a)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\partial B(r)}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu+l+1}{r}-\varepsilon\right)B(r)\right\}+\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\mu\tau}{r}\right)A(r)=0.

(444b)

Now we set
h_{1}(r)=A(r)+B(r)
and
h_{2}(r)=A(r)-B(r)
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\partial h_{1}(r)}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu+k_{1}}{r}\right)h_{1}(r)\right\}+\left(\frac{k_{2}-(l+1)}{r}\right)h_{2}(r)=0,

(445a)

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\partial h_{2}(r)}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu-k_{1}}{r}-2\varepsilon\right)h_{2}(r)\right\}+\left(\frac{-k_{2}-(l+1)}{r}\right)h_{1}(r)=0,

(445b)

where

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right)\,\,,\,\,k_{2}=\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\mu+\frac{1}{\mu}\right).

(446)

Now we set

\displaystyle h_{1}(r)=\sum_{K=0}^{\infty}a_{K}r^{K}=a_{0}+a_{1}r+\dots+a_{K}r^{K}+\dots,

\displaystyle h_{2}(r)=\sum_{K=0}^{\infty}b_{K}r^{K}=b_{0}+b_{1}r+\dots+b_{K}r^{K}+\dots,

(447)

then we have

\displaystyle(K+\nu+k_{1})a_{K}+(k_{2}-l-1)b_{K}=0,

(448a)

\displaystyle(K+\nu-k_{1})b_{K}-2\varepsilon b_{K-1}-(k_{2}+l+1)a_{K}=0.

(448b)

Based on which, one can obtain

\displaystyle b_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+\nu)^{2}-k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-(l+1)^{2}}b_{K-1}=\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+\nu)^{2}+\tau^{2}-(l+1)^{2}}b_{K-1}=\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+\nu)^{2}-\nu^{2}}b_{K-1}

(449)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+2\nu)K}b_{K-1}.

Similarly, from

\displaystyle(K+\nu+k_{1})a_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(k_{2}-l-1)b_{K}=-(k_{2}-l-1)\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+2\nu)K}b_{K-1}=\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+2\nu)K}\left[-(k_{2}-l-1)b_{K-1}\right]

(450)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+2\nu)K}\left[(K-1+\nu+k_{1})a_{K-1}\right]=(K+\nu+k_{1})\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1}-1)}{(K+2\nu)K}a_{K-1},

one has

\displaystyle a_{K}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1}-1)}{(K+2\nu)K}a_{K-1}.

(451)

The above results implies that

\displaystyle h_{1}(r)={D_{1}}\,F(\nu+k_{1};\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon{r})\,\,,\,\,h_{2}(r)={D_{2}}\,F(\nu+k_{1}+1;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon{r}).

(452)

Based on Eq. (
448a
), we have

\displaystyle(K+\nu+k_{1}){D_{1}}\frac{(\nu+k_{1})_{K}}{(2\nu+1)_{K}\,K!}=-(k_{2}-l-1){D_{2}}\frac{(\nu+k_{1}+1)_{K}}{(2\nu+1)_{K}\,K!},

(453)

with
(\gamma)_{K}=\gamma(\gamma+1)\cdots(\gamma+K-1)
. From Eq. (
453
) we have

\displaystyle(K+\nu+k_{1}){D_{1}}{(\nu+k_{1})_{K}}=-(k_{2}-l-1){D_{2}}{(\nu+k_{1}+1)_{K}},

(454)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(K+\nu+k_{1}){D_{1}}{(\nu+k_{1})}=-(k_{2}-l-1){D_{2}}{(\nu+k_{1}+K)},

(455)

i.e.,

\displaystyle{D_{1}}=-\frac{k_{2}-l-1}{\nu+k_{1}}{D_{2}}.

(456)

To ensure the radial wavefunction is not divergent when
r\rightarrow\infty
,
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
must be truncated. Thus, we let

\displaystyle\nu+k_{1}=-N,\,\,N=0,1,2,\dots.

(457)

In this situation,

\displaystyle h_{1}(r)={D_{1}}\,F(-N;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon{r})\,\,,\,\,h_{2}(r)={D_{2}}\,F(-N+1;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon{r}).

(458)

Further, we can have

\displaystyle F(r)={\rm i}\mu A(r)r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}={\rm i}\mu\dfrac{h_{1}(r)+h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r},

\displaystyle G(r)=B(r)r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}=\dfrac{h_{1}(r)-h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r},

(459)

or

\displaystyle F(r)=\frac{D_{1}}{2}{\rm i}\mu\,r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}\left\{F(-N;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon r)-\frac{\nu+k_{1}}{k_{2}-l-1}F(-N+1;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon r)\right\},

\displaystyle G(r)=\frac{D_{2}}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}\left\{F(-N;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon r)+\frac{\nu+k_{1}}{k_{2}-l-1}F(-N+1;\,2\nu+1;\,2\varepsilon r)\right\}.

(460)

Remark 12
.

Because

\displaystyle N+\nu+k_{1}=0,\,\,(N=0,1,2,\dots),

\displaystyle a_{K}=\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1}-1)}{(K+2\nu)K}a_{K-1},

\displaystyle b_{K}=\frac{2\varepsilon(K+\nu+k_{1})}{(K+2\nu)K}b_{K-1},

(461)

i.e.,

\displaystyle N+\nu+k_{1}=0,\,\,(N=0,1,2,\dots),

\displaystyle a_{N+1}=\frac{2\varepsilon(N+\nu+k_{1})}{(N+1+2\nu)(N+1)}a_{N},

\displaystyle b_{N}=\frac{2\varepsilon(N+\nu+k_{1})}{(N+2\nu)N}b_{N-1},

(462)

then for a fixed
N
, we have

\displaystyle a_{i}=0,\;\;(i\geq N+1),

\displaystyle b_{j}=0,\;\;(j\geq N),

(463)

thus

\displaystyle h_{1}(r)=\sum_{i=0}^{N}a_{i}r^{i},\;\;\;\;h_{2}(r)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}b_{j}r^{j}.

(464)

When
r\rightarrow\infty
, on one hand, from Eq. (
434
) we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{F}{G}=\dfrac{C_{1}e^{-\varepsilon r}}{C_{2}e^{-\varepsilon r}}=\dfrac{C_{1}}{C_{2}}={\rm i}\mu.

(465)

On one hand, from Eq. (
VI.1.1
) we have

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{F(r)}{G(r)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{{\rm i}\mu\dfrac{h_{1}(r)+h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}}{\dfrac{h_{1}(r)-h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}}={\rm i}\mu\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{h_{1}(r)+h_{2}(r)}{h_{1}(r)-h_{2}(r)}

(466)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\mu\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{\sum_{i=0}^{N}a_{i}r^{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}b_{j}r^{j}}{\sum_{i=0}^{N}a_{i}r^{i}-\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}b_{j}r^{j}}={\rm i}\mu\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\dfrac{a_{N}r^{N}}{a_{N}r^{N}}={\rm i}\mu.

Namely, Eq. (
466
) coincides with Eq. (
465
).

When
r\rightarrow 0
, on one hand, from Eq. (
439
) we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{F}{G}=\dfrac{C_{1}^{\prime}r^{\nu-1}}{C_{2}^{\prime}r^{\nu-1}}=\dfrac{C_{1}^{\prime}}{C_{2}^{\prime}}={\rm i}\sqrt{\dfrac{l+1+\nu}{l+1-\nu}}.

(467)

On one hand, from Eq. (
VI.1.1
) we have

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{F(r)}{G(r)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{{\rm i}\mu\dfrac{h_{1}(r)+h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}}{\dfrac{h_{1}(r)-h_{2}(r)}{2}r^{\nu-1}e^{-\varepsilon r}}={\rm i}\mu\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\dfrac{h_{1}(r)+h_{2}(r)}{h_{1}(r)-h_{2}(r)}

(468)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\mu\dfrac{D1+D_{2}}{D_{1}-D_{2}}={\rm i}\mu\dfrac{\dfrac{D1}{D_{2}}+1}{\dfrac{D1}{D_{2}}-1}={\rm i}\mu\dfrac{\left(-\dfrac{k_{2}-l-1}{\nu+k_{1}}\right)+1}{\left(-\dfrac{k_{2}-l-1}{\nu+k_{1}}\right)-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\mu\dfrac{-(k_{2}-l-1)+(\nu+k_{1})}{-(k_{2}-l-1)-(\nu+k_{1})}={\rm i}\mu\dfrac{(k_{2}-l-1)-(\nu+k_{1})}{(k_{2}-l-1)+(\nu+k_{1})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\mu\dfrac{(k_{2}-k_{1})-(l+1+\nu)}{(k_{2}+k_{1})-(l+1-\nu)}={\rm i}\mu\dfrac{\dfrac{\tau}{\mu}-(l+1+\nu)}{\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)}={\rm i}\dfrac{\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu}{\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)}.

Namely, to make Eq. (
468
) coincide with Eq. (
467
), one must have

\displaystyle\dfrac{\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu}{\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)}=\sqrt{\dfrac{l+1+\nu}{l+1-\nu}},

(469)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu=\sqrt{\dfrac{l+1+\nu}{l+1-\nu}}[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(470)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu=\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}-\nu^{2}}{(l+1-\nu)^{2}}}[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(471)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu=\dfrac{{|\tau|}}{l+1-\nu}[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(472)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\tau-(l+1+\nu)\mu=\dfrac{-\tau}{l+1-\nu}[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(473)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(l+1-\nu)\tau-[(l+1)^{2}-\nu^{2}]\mu=-\tau[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(474)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(l+1-\nu)\tau-\tau^{2}\mu=-\tau[\tau\mu-(l+1-\nu)],

(475)

which is an identity. Thus Eq. (
466
) coincides with Eq. (
465
).
\blacksquare

Next, let us come to determine the energy. Based on

\displaystyle\nu+k_{1}=-N,\,\,N=0,1,2,\dots,

\displaystyle k_{1}=\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right),

\displaystyle\mu=\sqrt{\frac{Mc^{2}+E}{Mc^{2}-E}},

(476)

we have

\displaystyle\mu^{2}=\frac{Mc^{2}+E}{Mc^{2}-E},

(477)

i.e.,

\displaystyle(\mu^{2}+1)E=(\mu^{2}-1)Mc^{2},

(478)

i.e.,

\displaystyle E=\dfrac{\mu^{2}-1}{\mu^{2}+1}Mc^{2}.

(479)

Here
\mu
is determined by

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right)=k_{1}=-(N+\nu),

(480)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right)=-\dfrac{2}{\tau}(N+\nu),

(481)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\mu^{2}+\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu-1=0.

(482)

By considering
\mu>0
, we then have

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{-\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\right)^{2}+4}}{2}=-\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{\tau}+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{\tau}\right)^{2}+1}

(483)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{|\tau|}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(N+\nu)^{2}}{\tau^{2}}+1}.

Due to Eq. (
481
) and Eq. (
482
), one obtains

\displaystyle\mu^{2}-1=-\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu,\;\;\;\;\mu^{2}+1=-\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu+2,\;\;\;\frac{1}{\mu}=\mu+\dfrac{2}{\tau}(N+\nu),

(484)

then

\displaystyle\dfrac{\mu^{2}-1}{\mu^{2}+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{-}\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu}{{-}\dfrac{2(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu+2}=\dfrac{-\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu}{-\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{\tau}\mu+1}=\dfrac{1}{1-\dfrac{\tau}{(N+\nu)}\dfrac{1}{\mu}}

(485)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{1-\dfrac{\tau}{(N+\nu)}\left[\mu+\dfrac{2}{\tau}(N+\nu)\right]}=\dfrac{1}{1+\dfrac{|\tau|}{(N+\nu)}\left[\mu-\dfrac{2}{|\tau|}(N+\nu)\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\dfrac{|\tau|}{(N+\nu)}\mu-1}=\dfrac{1}{\dfrac{|\tau|}{(N+\nu)}\left[\dfrac{(N+\nu)}{|\tau|}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(N+\nu)^{2}}{\tau^{2}}+1}\right]-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{\tau^{2}}{(N+\nu)^{2}}}}=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}}}.

Thus the energy is given by

\displaystyle E=\dfrac{\mu^{2}-1}{\mu^{2}+1}Mc^{2}=\dfrac{Mc^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}}}.

(486)

Remark 13
.

The Non-Relativistic Limit.
Because particle 1 has a mass
M
, thus the internal energy of
the particle 1 is
E_{\rm int}=Mc^{2}
. In the non-relativistic limit, after subtracting the internal energy, the external
energy of particle 1 is given by

\displaystyle E_{\rm ext}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E-E_{\rm int}=\dfrac{Mc^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}}}-Mc^{2}

(487)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle Mc^{2}\left(1-\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}\right)-Mc^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{Mq^{2}\kappa^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}=\dfrac{Mq^{2}\kappa^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{\left[N+\left(\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}}\right)\right]^{2}}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\dfrac{Mq^{2}\kappa^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+l+1)^{2}}.

After denoting
E_{\rm ext}
as
E_{n}
, and

\displaystyle n=N+l+1,

(488)

we then have

\displaystyle E_{n}=-\dfrac{M(q\kappa)^{2}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{n^{2}},\;\;(n=1,2,3,...),

(489)

which is just Eq. (
140
), thus reducing to the energy of the non-relativistic hydrogen-like atom.

Remark 14
.

The Relation between the Fine Structure Constant
\alpha_{\rm e}
and the Nuclear Charge Number
Z
.

It is well-known that the fine structure constant
\alpha_{\rm e}
is given by

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm e}=\dfrac{e^{2}}{\hbar c}\approx\dfrac{1}{137.0359}\approx\dfrac{1}{137},

(490)

which is a mysterious dimensionless number.

If considering
\kappa=Q=Ze
, then Eq. (
489
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle E_{n}=-\dfrac{Z^{2}Me^{4}}{2\hbar^{2}}\dfrac{1}{n^{2}}=-\dfrac{Z^{2}Mc^{2}}{2}\dfrac{e^{4}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{n^{2}}=-\dfrac{Z^{2}Mc^{2}}{2n^{2}}(\alpha_{\rm e})^{2},\;\;\;(n=1,2,3,...).

(491)

From which one may find that in the non-relativistic quantum mechanics, the nuclear charge number
Z
has nothing to do with the fine structure constant
\alpha_{\rm e}
. However, this situation is dramatically changed in the relativistic quantum mechanics. Let us focus on Eq. (
486
), the relativistic energy of hydrogen-like atom is given by

\displaystyle E=\dfrac{Mc^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+\nu)^{2}}}},

(492)

where
\nu
is given by

\displaystyle\nu=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\frac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}}=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\frac{(-e)^{2}(Ze)^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}}=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\frac{Z^{2}e^{4}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}}=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-Z^{2}(\alpha_{\rm e})^{2}}.

(493)

Here
\nu
contains a square-root, to make it be meaningful, one must have

\displaystyle(l+1)^{2}-Z^{2}(\alpha_{\rm e})^{2}\geq 0,

(494)

for any
l=0,1,2,\dots
. Because the minimum of
l
is
l_{\rm min}=0
, thus one must have

\displaystyle 1-Z^{2}(\alpha_{\rm e})^{2}\geq 0,

(495)

i.e.,

\displaystyle Z\leq\dfrac{1}{\alpha_{\rm e}}\approx 137.0359.

(496)

Based on this finding, Richard Feynman asserted that the biggest number of
Z
in the periodic table of elements is
Z=137
.
\blacksquare

VI.1.2
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{2}=0

In this case, Dirac’s equation with spin potential can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}_{1}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(497)

here
\kappa_{3}
has been denoted by
\kappa
for simplicity.

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables.
Alternatively, one may re-write the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}+\mathcal{H}_{I},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta_{1}mc^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{r},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boxed{\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})},

(498)

where the term
\mathcal{H}_{I}
is treated as an additional interaction based on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

In the following, we would like to list some possible formulas, which might be useful when one tries to exactly solve the eigen-problem.

Remark 15
.

Eigen-Problem of
(\hat{r}\cdot\sigma)
.
Here
\hat{r}=\vec{r}/r=(x,y,z)/r
. According to quantum textbook, the eigenvlaues of
(\hat{r}\cdot\sigma)
are
+1
and
-1
. In the
r\theta\phi
-coordinate, the eigenstates are (up to a phase factor)

\displaystyle\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2[1+(z/r)]}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1+(z/r)\\
(x+{\rm i}y)/r\\
\end{array}\right)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2(1+\cos\theta)}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1+\cos\theta\\
\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi}\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2[1+(z/r)]}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(x-{\rm i}y)/r\\
-[1+(z/r)]\\
\end{array}\right)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2(1+\cos\theta)}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
-(1+\cos\theta)\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\\
\end{array}\right).

In this work, we choose the eigenstates as (by selecting an appropriate phase)

\displaystyle\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right).

Thus, for the operator
(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})
, one has

\displaystyle(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})=+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})={-\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})}.

(519)

\blacksquare

Remark 16
.

Acting
\vec{p}=(p_{x},p_{y},p_{z})
acting on
\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})
and
\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})
. One can have

\displaystyle p_{x}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\sin\theta\cos\phi\,\partial_{r}+\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}-\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi}\right]\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\frac{1}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\frac{1}{2}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{{\rm i}}{2}\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right).

From

\displaystyle a\cos\frac{\theta}{2}+b\sin\frac{\theta}{2}=-\cos\frac{\theta}{2},

\displaystyle a\sin\frac{\theta}{2}-b\cos\frac{\theta}{2}=\sin\frac{\theta}{2},

(538)

we have

\displaystyle(a+1)\cos\frac{\theta}{2}+b\sin\frac{\theta}{2}=0,

\displaystyle(a-1)\sin\frac{\theta}{2}-b\cos\frac{\theta}{2}=0,

(539)

then

\displaystyle a=-\cos\theta,\;\;\;b=-\sin\theta.

(540)

We can have

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)=-\cos\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\sin\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r}).

thus

\displaystyle p_{x}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{{\rm i}}{2}\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

(549)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})+{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{{\rm i}}{2}\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left[-\cos\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\sin\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left[\cos\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})+\sin\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\left[{\rm i}\cos\theta\cos\phi+\sin\phi\right]\xi_{-1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle p_{x}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\sin\theta\cos\phi\,\partial_{r}+\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}-\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi}\right]\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

(558)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{2}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\frac{1}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left[\sin\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\cos\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r\sin\theta}\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\left[-{\rm i}\cos\theta\cos\phi+\sin\phi\right]\xi_{+1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle p_{y}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{-\rm i}\hbar\left[\sin\theta\sin\phi\,\partial_{r}+\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}+\frac{\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\partial_{\phi}\right]\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

(567)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{-\rm i}\hbar\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{2r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+\hbar\frac{\cos\phi}{2r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left[-\cos\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\sin\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})+\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\left[{\rm i}\cos\theta\sin\phi-\cos\phi\right]\xi_{-1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle p_{y}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{-\rm i}\hbar\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{2r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)+\hbar\frac{\cos\phi}{2r\sin\theta}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

(573)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{{\rm i}\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\sin\phi}{r}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\,\left[\sin\theta\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})-\cos\theta\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\frac{\cos\theta\cos\phi}{r\sin\theta}\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})-\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\dfrac{1}{r}\left[{\rm i}\cos\theta\sin\phi+\cos\phi\right]\xi_{+1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle p_{z}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\cos\theta\,\partial_{r}-\frac{\sin\theta}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}\right]\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)={\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{2r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)={-\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{2r}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r}),

\displaystyle p_{z}\,\xi_{-1}(\hat{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\left[\cos\theta\,\partial_{r}-\frac{\sin\theta}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}\right]\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)={\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{r}\,\partial_{\theta}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
-\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{2r}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\,e^{{\rm i}\phi/2}\\
\end{array}\right)={\rm i}\hbar\frac{\sin\theta}{2r}\,\xi_{+1}(\hat{r}).

\blacksquare

Remark 17
.

There are some useful recursive formulas for the spherical harmonics functions:

\displaystyle\cos\theta\,Y_{lm}=a_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m}+a_{l-1,m}Y_{l-1,m},

(590a)

\displaystyle\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\,\phi}Y_{lm}=b_{l-1,-(m+1)}Y_{l-1,m+1}-b_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m+1},

(590b)

\displaystyle\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\,\phi}Y_{lm}=-b_{l-1,m-1}Y_{l-1,m-1}+b_{l,-m}Y_{l+1,m-1},

(590c)

with

a_{l,m}\equiv\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+1)^{2}-m^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}=\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}},\qquad b_{l,m}\equiv\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}.

(591)

For the first few spherical harmonics, we have

\displaystyle Y_{00}=\sqrt{\dfrac{1}{4\pi}},

\displaystyle Y_{10}=\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{4\pi}}\,\cos\theta,

\displaystyle Y_{1\pm 1}=\mp\sqrt{\dfrac{3}{8\pi}}\,\sin\theta e^{\pm{\rm i}\phi},

\displaystyle Y_{20}=\sqrt{\dfrac{5}{16\pi}}\,\left(3\cos^{2}\theta-1\right),

\displaystyle Y_{2\pm 1}=\mp\sqrt{\dfrac{15}{8\pi}}\,\cos\theta\sin\theta e^{\pm{\rm i}\phi},

\displaystyle Y_{2\pm 2}=\sqrt{\dfrac{15}{32\pi}}\,\sin^{2}\theta e^{\pm{\rm i}2\phi}.

(592)

Based on which, one may verify the above recursive formulas.
\blacksquare

Remark 18
.

One can use the above recursive formulas to prove the following results:

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{A}_{lj_{1}m_{1}}=-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}m_{1}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}m_{1}}=-\Phi^{A}_{lj_{1}m_{1}},

(593)

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}=\hat{r}\cdot\vec{\sigma}=\begin{bmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
\sin\theta\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}&-\cos\theta\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{B}_{ljm_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}.

(594)

Proof.—
Because

\displaystyle\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{lm}+\sqrt{l-m}\,\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l,m+1}

(595)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\left(a_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m}+a_{l-1,m}Y_{l-1,m}\right)+\sqrt{l-m}\,\big[-b_{l-1,m}Y_{l-1,m}+b_{l,-(m+1)}Y_{l+1,m}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,a_{l,m}+\sqrt{l-m}\,b_{l,-(m+1)}\big]Y_{l+1,m}+\big[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,a_{l-1,m}-\sqrt{l-m}\,b_{l-1,m}\big]Y_{l-1,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)^{2}(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)^{2}(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+1,m}

\displaystyle+\Bigg[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m)(l-m)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}-\sqrt{l-m}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m)(l+m+1)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}\Bigg]Y_{l-1,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)^{2}(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)^{2}(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+1,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big[\sqrt{(l+m+1)^{2}}+\sqrt{(l-m)^{2}}\Big]Y_{l+1,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big(2l+1\Big)Y_{l+1,m},

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\phi}Y_{lm}-\sqrt{l-m}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l,m+1}

(596)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\big[b_{l-1,-(m+1)}Y_{l-1,m+1}-b_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m+1}\big]-\sqrt{l-m}\,(a_{l,m+1}Y_{l+1,m+1}+a_{l-1,m+1}Y_{l-1,m+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,b_{l-1,-(m+1)}-\sqrt{l-m}\,a_{l-1,m+1}\big]Y_{l-1,m+1}-\big(\sqrt{l+m+1}\,b_{l,m}+\sqrt{l-m}\,a_{l,m+1}\big)Y_{l+1,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m-1)(l-m)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}-\sqrt{l-m}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m-1)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}\Bigg]Y_{l-1,m+1}

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{l-m}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+1,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m-1)(l-m)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)(l+m+1)(l-m-1)}{(2\,l-1)(2\,l+1)}}\Bigg]Y_{l-1,m+1}

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)^{2}(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l-m)^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+1,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)^{2}(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l-m)^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+1,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big[\sqrt{(l+m+1)^{2}}+\sqrt{(l-m)^{2}}\Big]Y_{l+1,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big(2l+1\Big)Y_{l+1,m+1},

then one has

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
\sin\theta\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}&-\cos\theta\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\,Y_{lm}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}

(597)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}&\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{lm}+\sqrt{l-m}\,\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l,m+1}\\
&\sqrt{l+m+1}\,\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}Y_{lm}-\sqrt{l-m}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l,m+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}&\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big(2l+1\Big)Y_{l+1,m}\\
&-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\Big(2l+1\Big)Y_{l+1,m+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m+1}\>Y_{l+1,m}(\theta,\phi)\\
\sqrt{l+m+2}\>Y_{l+1,m+1}(\theta,\phi)\\
\end{bmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Phi^{B}_{l+1,jm_{j}}.

Likewise, because

\displaystyle-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l+1,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l+1,m+1}

(598)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,(a_{l+1,m}Y_{l+2,m}+a_{l,m}Y_{l,m})+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\big[-b_{l,m}Y_{l,m}+b_{l+1,-(m+1)}Y_{l+2,m}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,a_{l+1,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,b_{l+1,-(m+1)}\big]Y_{l+2,m}-(\sqrt{l-m+1}\,a_{l,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,b_{l,m})Y_{l,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg[-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l-m+2)}{(2\,l+3)(2\,l+5)}}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m+1)(l-m+2)}{(2\,l+3)(2\,l+5)}}\Bigg]Y_{l+2,m}

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\Bigg]Y_{l,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big[\sqrt{(l+m+2)^{2}}+\sqrt{(l-m+1)^{2}}\big]Y_{l,m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big(2l+3\big)Y_{l,m},

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l+1,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l+1,m+1}

(599)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\big[b_{l,-(m+1)}Y_{l,m+1}-b_{l+1,m}Y_{l+2,m+1}\big]+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,(a_{l+1,m+1}Y_{l+2,m+1}+a_{l,m+1}Y_{l,m+1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[\sqrt{l-m+1}\,b_{l,-(m+1)}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,a_{l,m+1}\big]Y_{l,m+1}+\bigl(-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,b_{l+1,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,a_{l+1,m+1}\bigr)Y_{l+2,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\right]Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle+\left[-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)(l+m+3)}{(2\,l+3)(2\,l+5)}}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+3)(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+3)(2\,l+5)}}\right]Y_{l+2,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)(l-m+1)^{2}}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}+\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+2)^{2}(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\right]Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big[\sqrt{(l+m+2)^{2}}+\sqrt{(l-m+1)^{2}}\big]Y_{l,m+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big(2l+3\big)Y_{l,m+1},

then we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{r}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,jm_{j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\,l+3}}\begin{bmatrix}\cos\theta&\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
\sin\theta\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}&-\cos\theta\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,Y_{l+1,m}\\
\sqrt{l+m+2}\,Y_{l+1,m+1}\end{bmatrix}

(600)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\begin{bmatrix}&-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l+1,m}+\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{-{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l+1,m+1}\\
&-\sqrt{l-m+1}\,\sin\theta\,\mathrm{e}^{{\rm i}\phi}\,Y_{l+1,m}-\sqrt{l+m+2}\,\cos\theta\,Y_{l+1,m+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\begin{bmatrix}&-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big[2l+3\big]Y_{l,m}\\
&-\sqrt{\dfrac{(l-m)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}}\,\big[2l+3\big]Y_{l,m+1}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{\dfrac{1}{\sqrt{2l+1}}\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt{l+m+1}\>Y_{l,m}\\
\sqrt{l-m}\>Y_{l,m+1}\end{bmatrix}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Phi^{A}_{ljm_{j}}.

This ends the proof.
\blacksquare

VI.1.3
\kappa_{1}=0
and
\kappa_{2}\neq 0

In this case, the Dirac equation with spin potential can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}_{1}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\boxed{\left(q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(601)

here
\kappa_{3}
has been denoted by
\kappa
for simplicity.

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

VI.1.4
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{2}\neq 0

In this case, the Dirac equation with spin potential can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\vec{\alpha}_{1}\cdot\vec{p}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\boxed{\left(\dfrac{q\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+q\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\,(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2})}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(602)

here
\kappa_{3}
has been denoted by
\kappa
for simplicity.

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved because one cannot find an appropriate common set of physical observables.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

VI.2
The Type-II Case

In this case, one has the parameters
g\neq 0,k\neq 0
, and
g\hbar k=1
. The Dirac-type Hamiltonian is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+q\varphi=\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\left(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S_{2}})+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(603)

with

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}_{2}}{r^{2}}.

(604)

Here we have denoted
\kappa_{3}
as
\kappa
(
\kappa=Q=Ze
,
Z
is an integer,
Z=1
for the hydrogen atom and
Z\geq 2
for the hydrogen-like atom).
For simplicity, we consider merely the spin-
1/2
case, i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{S_{2}}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\vec{\sigma}_{2},

(605)

which is written in
2\times 2
matrix representation.

VI.2.1
The Eigenfunctions of
{\hat{r}}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})

First we have known that

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha}_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\vec{\sigma}_{1}\\
\vec{\sigma}_{1}&0\end{bmatrix}=\sigma_{x}\otimes\vec{\sigma}_{1},\;\;\;\;\beta_{1}=\begin{bmatrix}\openone_{1}&0\\
0&-\openone_{1}\end{bmatrix}=\sigma_{z}\otimes\openone_{1},

(606)

We designate the “total” angular momentum operator as

\vec{J}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}_{1}+\vec{S}_{2},

(607)

where
\vec{\ell}
is the orbital angular momentum operator for particle 1, and

\vec{S}_{1}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\begin{bmatrix}\vec{\sigma}_{1}&0\\
0&\vec{\sigma}_{1}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{\hbar}{2}\left(\openone\otimes\vec{\sigma}_{1}\right)

(608)

is the spin-
1/2
angular momentum operator for particle 1, which is written in
4\times 4
matrix representation. One can prove that

[\vec{{r}}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}),\vec{J}]=0,

(609)

or

[\hat{{r}}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}),\vec{J}]=0,

(610)

with
\hat{r}=\vec{r}/r
.

Proof.

According to
1991Lange
, there are some useful properties about vectors and scalars.

Property (i).
Let
\vec{v}_{1}=(v_{1x},v_{1y},v_{1z})
and
\vec{v}_{2}=(v_{2x},v_{2y},v_{2z})
be two vectors with respect to the operator
\vec{\mathcal{L}}=(\mathcal{L}_{1x},\mathcal{L}_{1y},\mathcal{L}_{1z})
, then we have

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{\alpha},\ v_{1\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}v_{1\gamma},

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{\alpha},\ v_{2\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}v_{2\gamma}.

(611)

Then for

\displaystyle\vec{v}=\vec{v}_{1}\times\vec{v}_{2},

(612)

it is also a vector with respect to
\vec{\mathcal{L}}
and it satisfies

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}\,,(\vec{v}_{1}\times\vec{v}_{2})_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\vec{v}_{1}\times\vec{v}_{2})_{\gamma}.

(613)

For examples, the position operator
\vec{r}
and the momentum operator
\vec{p}
are vectors with respect to the orbital angular momentum operator
\vec{\ell}=(\ell_{x},\ell_{y},\ell_{z})
, i.e., they satisfied

\displaystyle[\ell_{\alpha},\ r_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}r_{\gamma},

\displaystyle[\ell_{\alpha},\ p_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}p_{\gamma},

(614)

then
\vec{\ell}=\vec{r}\times\vec{p}
is also a vector with respect to
\vec{\ell}
, because

\displaystyle[\ell_{\alpha},\ \ell_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\ell_{\gamma}.

(615)

Similarly, the spin operator
\vec{S}
is a vector with respective to itself because

\displaystyle[S_{\alpha},\ S_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}S_{\gamma},

(616)

and the “total” angular momentum operator
\vec{J}
is also a vector with respective to itself, because

\displaystyle[J_{\alpha},\ J_{\beta}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}J_{\gamma}.

(617)

Property (ii).
Let
\vec{v}_{1}
and
\vec{v}_{2}
be two vectors with respect to
\vec{\mathcal{L}}
, then one can easily prove that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}=\vec{v}_{1}\cdot\vec{v}_{2}

(618)

is a scalar with respect to
\vec{\mathcal{L}}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle[\mathcal{L}_{\alpha},\ \mathcal{K}]=0.

(619)

For examples,
\vec{r}\cdot\vec{r}
,
\vec{p}\cdot\vec{p}
,
\vec{\ell}\cdot\vec{\ell}
,
\vec{r}\cdot\vec{p}
,

\vec{r}\cdot\vec{\ell}
,
\vec{p}\cdot\vec{\ell}
are scalars with respect to
\vec{\ell}
.

Now we come to prove Eq. (
609
). It is obvious that

\displaystyle[J_{i},\alpha_{1j}]={\rm i}\epsilon_{ijk}\alpha_{k}\,\,,\,\,[J_{i},\sigma_{2j}]={\rm i}\epsilon_{ijk}\sigma_{2k}\,\,,\,\,[J_{i},r_{i}]={\rm i}\epsilon_{ijk}r_{k},

(620)

namely,
\vec{\alpha}
,
\vec{\sigma}_{2}
, and
\vec{r}
are vectors with respect to
\vec{J}
. Then, according to property (i), we have

\displaystyle[J_{i},(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma_{2}})_{j}]={\rm i}\epsilon_{ijk}(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma_{2}})_{k},

(621)

i.e.,
(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma_{2}})
is a vector with respect to
\vec{J}
. Furthermore, according to property (ii), we have

[\vec{J},\,\vec{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})]=0.

(622)

Since
r
is a scalar with respect to
\vec{J}
, i.e.,
[\vec{J},\vec{r}\cdot\vec{r}]=[\vec{J},r^{2}]=0
,
[\vec{J},r]=0
,
[\vec{J},1/r]=0
,
then one easily has

[\vec{J},\,\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})]=0.

(623)

This ends the proof.
∎

One may verify that

[\vec{J},\mathcal{H}]=0,\;\;\;[\vec{J}^{\,2},\mathcal{H}]=0,\;\;\;[\vec{J}^{\,2},\vec{J}]=0,

(624)

which means that
\vec{J}
is a conversed quantity for the Hamiltonian system of
\mathcal{H}
. Then one can choose

\{\mathcal{H},\vec{J}^{\,2},J_{z}\}
as a common set to study the eigen-problem.

For
\vec{J}
, it is coupled by
\vec{\ell}
,
\vec{S_{1}}
, and
\vec{S_{2}}
. For simplicity, we can designate
the “total” angular momentum operator for particle 1 as
\vec{J}_{1}
, i.e.,

\vec{J}_{1}=\vec{\ell}+\vec{S}_{1},

(625)

then

\vec{J}=\vec{J_{1}}+\vec{S}_{2}.

(626)

\vec{J}
can be regarded as the result of the coupling of
\vec{J_{1}}
and
\vec{S_{2}}
, we then have the eigenstates of
\{\vec{J}^{\,2},J_{z}\}
as

\displaystyle\vec{J}^{\,2}|j,m_{j}\rangle=j(j+1)\hbar^{2}\,|j,m_{j}\rangle,

\displaystyle J_{z}|j,m_{j}\rangle=m_{j}\hbar\,|j,m_{j}\rangle,

(627)

where

\left|j,m_{j}\right\rangle=A\left|j_{1},m_{1},\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle+B\left|j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle+C\left|j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1},\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle+D\left|j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime},-\frac{1}{2}\right\rangle,

(628)

j_{1}=j-\frac{1}{2}
,
j_{1}^{\prime}=j+\frac{1}{2}
,
m_{1}=m_{j}-\frac{1}{2}
,
m^{\prime}_{1}=m_{j}+\frac{1}{2}
, and
A,B,C,D
are some coefficients.

For convenience, let us denote
\hat{r}=\vec{r}/r=(\hat{x},\hat{y},\hat{z})
, we then have

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}=\openone\otimes\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2},

(629)

where

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}=\left[\hat{x}\left({\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2z}}-{\sigma_{1z}}{\sigma_{2y}}\right)+\hat{y}\left({\sigma_{1z}}{\sigma_{2x}}-{\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2z}}\right)+\hat{z}\left({\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2y}}-{\sigma_{y}}_{1}{\sigma_{x}}_{2}\right)\right]^{2}.

(630)

To determine the eigenvalues of
\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}
, it is sufficient to rotate
\hat{r}
to
\hat{z}
. Namely,
\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}
and
\left[\hat{n}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}
share the same eigenvalues with
\hat{n}=(0,0,1)
. One can have

\displaystyle\left[\hat{n}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2y}}-{\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2x}}\right)^{2}=2(\openone\times\openone)-\left[({\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2y}})({\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2x}})+({\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2x}})({\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2y}})\right]

(631)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\openone\times\openone)-\left[({\rm i})(-{\rm i})(\sigma_{1z}\otimes\sigma_{2z})+(-{\rm i})({\rm i})(\sigma_{1z}\otimes\sigma_{2z})\right]=2\left(\openone\times\openone-\sigma_{1z}\otimes\sigma_{2z}\right),

thus the eigenvalues of
\left[\hat{n}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]^{2}
are 4 or 0.
This implies the eigenvalues of
\left[\hat{n}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]
are 2,
-2
, and 0.

Now we can designate the common eigenfunctions of
\{\vec{J}^{\,2},J_{z},\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\}
as

\displaystyle\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\\
a_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\end{pmatrix}_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}_{2}

(632)

\displaystyle+\begin{pmatrix}b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
b_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}_{2},

We have

\displaystyle\vec{J}^{\,2}\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=j(j+1)\hbar^{2}\,\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

\displaystyle J_{z}\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=m_{j}\hbar\,\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

(633)

and

\displaystyle\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)=\eta\,\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi),

(634)

with

\displaystyle\eta=2,\;\;-2,\;\;{\rm or}\;\;\;0,

(635)

i.e.,
\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})
has eight eigenvalues, two of them are 2, two of them are
-2
, and the rest four are 0.

Let

\displaystyle x=r\sin\theta\cos\phi,\;\;\;y=r\sin\theta\sin\phi,\;\;\;z=r\cos\theta,

(636)

we can attain

\displaystyle\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left({\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2z}}-{\sigma_{1z}}{\sigma_{2y}}\right)\sin\theta\cos\phi+\left({\sigma_{1z}}{\sigma_{2x}}-{\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2z}}\right)\sin\theta\sin\phi+\left({\sigma_{1x}}{\sigma_{2y}}-{\sigma_{1y}}{\sigma_{2x}}\right)\cos\theta.

(637)

From Eq. (
634
) we have

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]\biggr(a_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}_{2}

(638)

\displaystyle+\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]\biggr(b_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\,\Biggr[\biggr(a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}_{2}

\displaystyle+\biggr(b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}_{2}\Biggr],

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]\biggr(a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}_{2}

(639)

\displaystyle+\left[\hat{r}\cdot(\vec{\sigma}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})\right]\biggr(b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\,\Biggr[\biggr(a^{\prime}_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a^{\prime}_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a^{\prime}_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a^{\prime}_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}_{2}

\displaystyle+\biggr(b^{\prime}_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b^{\prime}_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b^{\prime}_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b^{\prime}_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\biggr)_{1}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}_{2}\Biggr].

Due to the following relations

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},\;\;\;\sigma_{x}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\sigma_{y}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}={\rm i}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},\;\;\;\sigma_{y}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}=-{\rm i}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\sigma_{z}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix},\;\;\;\sigma_{z}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}=-\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

(640)

from Eq. (
638
) and Eq. (
639
) we directly obtain

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{1y}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi-\sigma_{1x}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi\right)\left(a_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\sin\phi-\sigma_{1y}\cos\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}\cos\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\cos\phi\right)\left(b_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\left(a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right),

(641)

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{1y}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi-\sigma_{1x}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi\right)\left(a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\sin\phi-\sigma_{1y}\cos\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}\cos\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\cos\phi\right)\left(b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\left(a_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right),

(642)

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{1y}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi-\sigma_{1x}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi\right)\left(b_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{1z}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi-\sigma_{1y}{\cos}\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}{\cos}\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi\right)\left(a_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\left(b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right),

(643)

\displaystyle-\left(\sigma_{1y}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi-\sigma_{1x}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi\right)\left(b_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\sigma_{1z}{\sin}\theta{\sin}\phi-\sigma_{1y}{\cos}\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}{\cos}\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}{\sin}\theta{\cos}\phi\right)\left(a_{1}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{2}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}+a_{3}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+a_{4}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\eta\left(b_{1}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{2}^{\prime}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{3}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+b_{4}^{\prime}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right).

(644)

Through the calculation, we acquire

\displaystyle\sigma_{1y}\sin\theta\cos\phi-\sigma_{1x}\sin\theta\sin\phi=-{\rm i}\sin\theta\begin{pmatrix}0&e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\\
-e^{{\rm i}\phi}&0\end{pmatrix},

(645)

\displaystyle\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\sin\phi-\sigma_{1y}\cos\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}\cos\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\cos\phi={\rm i}\,\sin\theta e^{-{\rm i}\phi}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}-\rm i\,\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
2&0\end{pmatrix},

(646)

\displaystyle\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\sin\phi-\sigma_{1y}\cos\theta+{\rm i}\sigma_{1x}\cos\theta-{\rm i}\sigma_{1z}\sin\theta\cos\phi=-{\rm i}\,\sin\theta e^{{\rm i}\phi}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}+{\rm i}\,\cos\theta\begin{pmatrix}0&2\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}.

(647)

1.1. The Case of
\eta=2
or
