Remark 19
.

For Eq. (
VI.2.1
),
it requires

\displaystyle{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}-b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle+{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}+b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}+b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\,\eta\left\{a_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+1}{2l+1}}Y_{lm}+a_{2}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m}-a_{3}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m}-a_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+2}{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m}\right\},

(648)

and

\displaystyle-{\rm sin}\theta e^{\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m}+\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m}-\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m}-\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m}\right\}

\displaystyle+{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+2}+\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+2}\right\}

\displaystyle+2{\rm cos}\theta\left\{\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\,\eta\left\{a_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+a_{2}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+a_{3}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+a_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}.

(649)

Due to the following recursive formulas

\displaystyle\cos\theta\,Y_{lm}=a_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m}+a_{l-1,m}Y_{l-1,m},

(650a)

\displaystyle\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\,\phi}Y_{lm}=b_{l-1,-(m+1)}Y_{l-1,m+1}-b_{l,m}Y_{l+1,m+1},

(650b)

\displaystyle\sin\theta\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\,\phi}Y_{lm}=-b_{l-1,m-1}Y_{l-1,m-1}+b_{l,-m}Y_{l+1,m-1},

(650c)

with

a_{l,m}=\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l-m+1)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}},\qquad b_{l,m}=\sqrt{\dfrac{(l+m+1)(l+m+2)}{(2\,l+1)(2\,l+3)}},

(651)

from Eq. (
19
) we must require that

\displaystyle a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2},

\displaystyle a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1},

\displaystyle a_{2}\sqrt{{l+m+2}}=a_{3}\sqrt{{l-m+1}},

(652)

thus Eq. (
19
) yields

\displaystyle-\left\{\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}+b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right\}b_{lm}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+1}{2l+1}},

(653)

\displaystyle-\left\{\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}+b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right\}b_{l+1,-m-1}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+2}{2l+5}},

(654)

which leads to

\displaystyle\sqrt{l-m+1}\,a_{1}=\sqrt{l+m+2}\,a_{4}.

(655)

Eq. (
19
) indicates

-\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}b_{l-1,-m-1}-b_{l-1,m+1}\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}+2a_{l-1,m+1}\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}=0,

(656)

-b_{l+2,m}\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}+b_{l+2,-m-2}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}-2a_{l+2,m+1}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}=0,

(657)

which leads to

\displaystyle a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2},

\displaystyle a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}.

(658)

We can attain from Eq. (
19
)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}b_{l,m}+b_{l,-m-2}\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}+2a_{l,m+1}\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}+b_{l+1,-m-1}\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}

\displaystyle-b_{l+1,m+1}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}-2a_{l+1,m+1}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}={\rm i}\eta\,(a_{2}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{2l+3}}+a_{3}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{2l+3}}),

(659)

\displaystyle-b_{l,-m-1}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)-b_{l,m+1}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)

\displaystyle+2a_{l,m+1}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)={\rm i}\eta\,a_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{2l+1}},

(660)

\displaystyle b_{l+1,m}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)+b_{l+1,-m-2}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)

\displaystyle+2a_{l+1,m+1}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)={\rm i}\eta\,a_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{2l+5}}.

(661)

Based on Eq. (
19
), we can easily attain

\displaystyle\frac{2(l+1)a_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{(l+m+2)(2l+3)}}+\frac{2(l+2)a_{4}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{(l-m+1)(2l+3)}}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2l+3}{l-m+1}}.

(662)

\blacksquare

Remark 20
.

For Eq. (
VI.2.1
)
it requires

\displaystyle{\rm sin}\theta e^{\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}-a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}-a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle{\rm sin}\theta e^{\rm i\phi}\left\{-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}+a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}+a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\,\eta\,\left\{b_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m-1}{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+2}+b_{2}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+b_{3}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+b_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+4}{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+2}\right\},

(663)

and

\displaystyle{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+2}+\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+2}+\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+2}\right\}

\displaystyle+2{\rm cos}\theta\left\{\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=-{\rm i}\,\eta\,\left\{b_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+b_{2}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-b_{3}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}-b_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}.

(664)

Based on Eq. (
20
), we can attain

\displaystyle{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}=a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}},

\displaystyle b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}=-a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3},

\displaystyle b_{2}\sqrt{{l-m}}=-b_{3}\sqrt{{l+m+3}},

(665)

and

\left\{\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}-a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}+a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right\}b_{l,-m-2}={\rm i}\,\eta\,\left\{b_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m-1}{2l+1}}\right\},

(666)

-\left\{\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}-a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}+a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right\}b_{l+1,m+1}={\rm i}\,\eta\,\left\{b_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+4}{2l+5}}\right\},

(667)

which means

\displaystyle b_{1}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-\sqrt{l-m}b_{4}.

(668)

Based on Eq. (
20
), we can attain

-\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}b_{l-1,m+1}-b_{l-1,-m-1}\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}+2a_{l-1,m+1}\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}=0,

(669)

b_{l+2,-m-2}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}-b_{l+2,m}\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}+2a_{l+2,m+1}\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}=0,

The results are equivalent to those shown in Eq. (

).

), we also can attain

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m-1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}b_{l,-m-2}+b_{l,m}\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}+2a_{l,m+1}\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}-b_{l+1,m+1}\frac{b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+4}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}

(672)

\displaystyle-b_{l,m+1}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)-b_{l,-m-1}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)

\displaystyle+2a_{l,m+1}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)=-{\rm i}\eta\,b_{1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{2l+1}},

(673)

\displaystyle b_{l+1,-m-2}\left(\frac{b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)+b_{l+1,m}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}-\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)

\displaystyle+2a_{l+1,m+1}\left(\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}+\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}\right)={\rm i}\eta\,b_{4}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{2l+1}}.

(674)

According to Eq. (
20
), we attain

\displaystyle\frac{2(l+1)a^{\prime}_{1}}{\sqrt{(l+m+2)(2l+3)}}+\frac{2(l+2)a^{\prime}_{4}}{\sqrt{(l-m+1)(2l+3)}}=-{\rm i}\eta\,b_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2l+3}{l+m+3}}.

(675)

For Eq. (
VI.2.1
), we can make transformation

\displaystyle a_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow a_{1}\,\,,\,a_{2}^{\prime}\rightarrow a_{2}\,\,,\,a_{3}^{\prime}\rightarrow a_{3}\,\,,\,a_{4}^{\prime}\rightarrow a_{4},

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\prime}\rightarrow b_{1}\,\,,\,b_{2}^{\prime}\rightarrow b_{2}\,\,,\,b_{3}^{\prime}\rightarrow b_{3}\,\,,\,b_{4}^{\prime}\rightarrow b_{4},

\displaystyle a_{1}\rightarrow a_{1}^{\prime}\,\,,\,a_{2}\rightarrow a_{2}^{\prime}\,\,,\,a_{3}\rightarrow a_{3}^{\prime}\,\,,\,a_{4}\rightarrow a_{4}^{\prime},

(676)

then we can attain the similar conclusion as the one from Eq. (
VI.2.1
), i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}=a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2},

\displaystyle a_{1}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}\sqrt{l+m+2},

\displaystyle a_{4}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}\sqrt{l-m+1},

\displaystyle a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{{l+m+2}}=a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{{l-m+1}}.

(677)

So Eq. (
662
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{2(l+1)a_{1}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{(l+m+2)(2l+3)}}+\frac{2(l+2)a_{4}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{(l-m+1)(2l+3)}}=2\sqrt{\frac{(2l+3)}{(l+m+2)}}a_{1}^{\prime}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2l+3}{l-m+1}},

(678)

and Eq. (
675
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{2(l+1)a^{\prime}_{1}}{\sqrt{(l+m+2)(2l+3)}}+\frac{2(l+2)a^{\prime}_{4}}{\sqrt{(l-m+1)(2l+3)}}=2\sqrt{\frac{(2l+3)}{(l+m+2)}}a_{1}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}\eta\,b_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2l+3}{l+m+3}},

(679)

which mean

\displaystyle a_{2}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{2}\sqrt{l-m+1}.

(680)

\blacksquare

Similarly, we can calculate Eq. (
VI.2.1
) like Eq. (
VI.2.1
), and as a result, we can attain

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{1}\sqrt{l-m+1}=a_{4}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{4}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
a_{2}\sqrt{l+m+2}=a_{3}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right.,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{2}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{2}\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
b_{2}\sqrt{l-m}=-b_{3}\sqrt{l+m+3}\\
b_{1}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}\sqrt{l-m}\\
2a_{1}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-{\rm i}\eta\,b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}\end{matrix}\right.,

(681a)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}=a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}=a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right.,\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=-b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}\\
b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}\\
2a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-{\rm i}\eta\,b_{2}\sqrt{l+m+2}\end{matrix}\right..

(681b)

Then for Eq. (
19
), we have

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}^{\prime}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{1}

(682)

and based on Eq. (
VI.2.1
), we also have

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}={\rm i}\eta\,a_{1}^{\prime}.

(683)

Analysis 1.—
Based on the above results, for
\eta=2
, we have the coefficients as

\displaystyle b_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;a_{4}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;b_{2}^{\prime}={\rm i}\frac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle a_{2}^{\prime}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle a_{3}^{\prime}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{2}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b_{3}^{\prime}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}^{\prime}=-{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;a^{\prime}_{4}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;b_{2}={\rm i}\frac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a^{\prime}_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle a_{2}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}=-{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle a_{3}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{2}=-{\rm i}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle b_{3}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}=-{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1}.

(684)

There are still two free coefficients (i.e.,
a_{1}
and
a^{\prime}_{1}
) in the above equation (the reason is that the
eigenvalue
\eta=2
is 2-fold degeneracy).
Based on Eq. (
VI.2.1
), one can verify that Eq. (
653
), Eq. (
654
), Eq. (
19
),
Eq. (
19
), Eq. (
20
), Eq. (
20
) are valid.

From Eq. (
632
), if we require the wavefunction
\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
is normalized, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}\left(|a_{j}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{j}|^{2}+|b_{j}|^{2}+|b^{\prime}_{j}|^{2}\right)=1,

(685)

then we have

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{l+m+2}\biggr\{\left[(1)+\left(l-m+1\right)+\left(l-m+1\right)+\left(1\right)+\left(l-m\right)+\left(l+m+3\right)+\left(l+m+3\right)+\left(l-m\right)\right]|a_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(l-m+1\right)+\left(1\right)+\left(1\right)+\left(l-m+1\right)+\left(l+m+3\right)+\left(l-m\right)+\left(l-m\right)+\left(l+m+3\right)\right]|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}\biggr\}=1,

(686)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\dfrac{3l-m+5}{l+m+2}\left(|a_{1}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}\right)=1,

(687)

i.e.,

\displaystyle|a_{1}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}=\dfrac{l+m+2}{3l-m+5}.

(688)

Analysis 2.—
Based on the above results, for
\eta=-2
, we have the coefficients as

\displaystyle b_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;a_{4}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;b_{2}^{\prime}={-}{\rm i}\frac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle a_{2}^{\prime}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}^{\prime}={+}{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle a_{3}^{\prime}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{2}^{\prime}={+}{\rm i}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b_{3}^{\prime}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}^{\prime}={+}{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;a^{\prime}_{4}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;b_{2}={-}{\rm i}\frac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a^{\prime}_{4}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle a_{2}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}={+}{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1}

\displaystyle a_{3}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{2}={+}{\rm i}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle b_{3}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2}={+}{\rm i}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1}.

(689)

There are still two free coefficients (i.e.,
a_{1}
and
a^{\prime}_{1}
) in the above equation (the reason is that the
eigenvalue
\eta=-2
is 2-fold degeneracy). Under the normalized condition, similarly, we have

\displaystyle|a_{1}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}=\dfrac{l+m+2}{3l-m+5}.

(690)

1.2. The Case of
\eta=0

Similarly, we have the constrained conditions for the coefficients as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{1}(l+1)\sqrt{l-m+1}=-a_{4}(l+2)\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{4}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right.,\;\;\;\;\;\left\{\begin{matrix}{b_{2}(l+2)\sqrt{l-m}=b_{3}(l+1)\sqrt{l+m+3}}\\
a_{2}(l+2)\sqrt{l+m+2}=-a_{3}(l+1)\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
a_{2}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{2}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right.,

(691a)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{1}^{\prime}(l+1)\sqrt{l-m+1}=-a_{4}^{\prime}(l+2)\sqrt{l+m+2}\\
a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right.,\;\;\;\;\;\left\{\begin{matrix}{b_{2}^{\prime}(l+2)\sqrt{l-m}=b_{3}^{\prime}(l+1)\sqrt{l+m+3}}\\
a_{2}^{\prime}(l+2)\sqrt{l+m+2}=-a_{3}^{\prime}(l+1)\sqrt{l-m+1}\\
a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}\end{matrix}\right..

(691b)

From the above equation, we can have

\displaystyle b_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;a_{4}=-\frac{l+1}{l+2}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},\;\;\;

\displaystyle b_{4}=\frac{l+1}{l+2}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a_{1},

\displaystyle b_{3}=\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2},\;\;\;a_{2}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2},

\displaystyle a_{3}=-\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a_{2}=\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b_{2},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{1}=\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;a^{\prime}_{4}=-\frac{l+1}{l+2}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},\;\;\;

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{4}=\frac{l+1}{l+2}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{l+m+2}}\,a^{\prime}_{1},

\displaystyle b^{\prime}_{3}=\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b^{\prime}_{2},\;\;\;a^{\prime}_{2}=-\dfrac{\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b^{\prime}_{2},

\displaystyle a^{\prime}_{3}=-\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l-m+1}}\,a^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{l+2}{l+1}\dfrac{\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{l+m+3}}\,b^{\prime}_{2}.

(692)

In the following, let us come to make a check. For example, for
\eta=0
, Eq. (
19
) becomes

\displaystyle{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}-b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}}{\sqrt{2l+1}}Y_{l,m+1}+\frac{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}\right\}

\displaystyle+{\rm sin}\theta e^{-\rm i\phi}\left\{\frac{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}+b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}{\sqrt{2l+3}}Y_{l+1,m+1}+\frac{a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}+b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}}{\sqrt{2l+5}}Y_{l+2,m+1}\right\}=0,

(693)

which is valid based on Eq. (
VI.2.1
). And for
\eta=0
, Eq. (
19
) requires

\displaystyle a_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}=b_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2},

\displaystyle a_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}=-b_{4}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1},

\displaystyle{a_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m+1}-b_{2}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+3}}+{a_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l+m+2}+b_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{l-m}}=0,

(694)

which is valid based on Eq. (
VI.2.1
). Similarly, one can check other related equations are also valid.

There are still four free coefficients (i.e.,
a_{1}
,
a^{\prime}_{1}
,
b_{2}
, and
b^{\prime}_{2}
) in Eq. (
VI.2.1
) (the reason is that the
eigenvalue
\eta=0
is 4-fold degeneracy). If we require the wavefunction
\Phi_{j,m_{j}}(\theta,\phi)
is normalized, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}\left(|a_{j}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{j}|^{2}+|b_{j}|^{2}+|b^{\prime}_{j}|^{2}\right)=1,

(695)

then we have

\displaystyle\biggr\{\left[\left(1\right)+\left(\frac{(l+1)^{2}}{(l+2)^{2}}\dfrac{{l-m+1}}{{l+m+2}}\right)+\left(\dfrac{{l-m}}{{l+m+2}}\right)+\left(\frac{(l+1)^{2}}{(l+2)^{2}}\dfrac{{l+m+3}}{{l+m+2}}\right)\right]|a_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(1\right)+\left(\frac{(l+1)^{2}}{(l+2)^{2}}\dfrac{{l-m+1}}{{l+m+2}}\right)+\left(\dfrac{{l-m}}{{l+m+2}}\right)+\left(\frac{(l+1)^{2}}{(l+2)^{2}}\dfrac{{l+m+3}}{{l+m+2}}\right)\right]|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(\dfrac{{l-m+1}}{{l+m+3}}\right)+\left(\frac{(l+2)^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}}\dfrac{{l+m+2}}{{l+m+3}}\right)+\left(1\right)+\left(\frac{(l+2)^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}}\dfrac{{l-m}}{{l+m+3}}\right)\right]|b_{2}|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(\dfrac{{l-m+1}}{{l+m+3}}\right)+\left(\frac{(l+2)^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}}\dfrac{{l+m+2}}{{l+m+3}}\right)+\left(1\right)+\left(\frac{(l+2)^{2}}{(l+1)^{2}}\dfrac{{l-m}}{{l+m+3}}\right)\right]|b^{\prime}_{2}|^{2}\biggr\}=1,

(696)

i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{2(l+1)(2l+3)}{(l+2)(l+m+2)}\right]\left(|a_{1}|^{2}+|a^{\prime}_{1}|^{2}\right)+\left[\frac{2(l+2)(2l+3)}{(l+1)(l+m+3)}\right]\left(|b_{2}|^{2}+|b^{\prime}_{2}|^{2}\right)=1.

(697)

VI.2.2
\kappa_{1}=0
and
\kappa_{3}\neq 0

In this case, based on Eq. (
603
), the Dirac equation turns to

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+q\varphi=\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\left(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(698)

with

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}_{2}}{r^{2}}.

(699)

and
\kappa_{3}
has been denoted as
\kappa
for simplicity. We then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}=c\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\left[\vec{p}-\frac{qk\hbar(\hat{r}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2})}{2cr}\right]+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\frac{q\kappa}{r},

(700)

and the eigen-problem is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\Psi_{j,m_{j}}(r,\theta,\phi)=E\,\Psi_{j,m_{j}}(r,\theta,\phi),

(701)

where
E
is the eigenvalue and
\Psi_{j,m_{j}}(r,\theta,\phi)
is the eigenfunction.

For convenience, we designate

\displaystyle\frac{E}{\hbar c}=\overline{E},\;\;\;\;\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar c}=\overline{M},\;\;\;\;\frac{qk}{2c}=\overline{k},\;\;\;\;\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}=\overline{\kappa}.

(702)

We now assume that the form of the eigenfunction
\Psi_{j,mj}(r,\theta,\phi)
is as follows
\Psi_{j,mj}(r,\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle\Psi_{j,mj}(r,\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[f_{1}(r)\Phi_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+f_{2}(r)\Phi_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+f_{3}(r)\Phi_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+f_{4}(r)\Phi_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)\right]

(703)

\displaystyle+\left[g_{1}(r)\Phi^{\prime}_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+g_{2}(r)\Phi^{\prime}_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+g_{3}(r)\Phi^{\prime}_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)+g_{4}(r)\Phi^{\prime}_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)\right],

where

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704a)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+2}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\\
{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704b)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+2}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\\
{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704c)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m+1}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}+\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704d)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m+1}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}-\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704e)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+2}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+\frac{l+1}{l+2}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}\sqrt{\frac{l-m+1}{l+m+2}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704f)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+2}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
-{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}+\frac{l+1}{l+2}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m}}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704g)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m+1}}\begin{pmatrix}\Phi^{A}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}^{\prime}}+\frac{l+2}{l+1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{B}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\rm i}\left\{{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}\sqrt{\frac{l+m+2}{l-m+1}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}1\\
0\end{pmatrix}

(704h)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{l+m+3}{l-m+1}}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\rm i}\left\{\Phi^{B}_{l+1,j_{1}^{\prime},m_{1}}+\frac{l+2}{l+1}\sqrt{\frac{l-m}{l+m+3}}\Phi^{A}_{l,j_{1},m_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}\end{pmatrix}\otimes\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix},

and

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\,\Phi_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=2\Phi_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi),\;\;\;\;\;\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi^{\prime}_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=-2\Phi^{\prime}_{1,l,m}(\theta,\phi),

(705a)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=2\Phi_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi),\;\;\;\;\;\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi^{\prime}_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=-2\Phi^{\prime}_{2,l,m}(\theta,\phi),

(705b)

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=0,\;\;\;\;\;\ \left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi^{\prime}_{3,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=0,\;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=0,\;\;\;\;\;\left[\hat{r}\cdot\left(\vec{\alpha}_{1}\times\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)\right]\Phi^{\prime}_{4,l,m}(\theta,\phi)=0.

(705c)

Then we can attain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{1}-g_{1}+g_{3})}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}(f_{1}-g_{1}+g_{3})+\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{1}-g_{1})+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}+\overline{M}\right)(f_{1}+g_{1}+f_{3})=0,

(706a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{1}+g_{1}+f_{3})}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}(f_{1}+g_{1}+f_{3})-\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{1}+g_{1})-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}-\overline{M}\right)(f_{1}-g_{1}+g_{3})=0,

(706b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{1}-g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}g_{3})}{\partial r}-\frac{l+1}{r}\left(f_{1}-g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}g_{3}\right)+\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{1}-g_{1})+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}+\overline{M}\right)\left(f_{1}+g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}f_{3}\right)=0,

(706c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{1}+g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}f_{3})}{\partial r}+\frac{l+3}{r}\left(f_{1}+g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}f_{3}\right)-\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{1}+g_{1})-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}-\overline{M}\right)\left(f_{1}-g_{1}-\frac{l+1}{l+2}g_{3}\right)=0,

(706d)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{2}-g_{2}+g_{4})}{\partial r}+\frac{l+3}{r}(f_{2}-g_{2}+g_{4})-\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{2}-g_{2})-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}+\overline{M}\right)(f_{2}+g_{2}+f_{4})=0,

(707a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{2}+g_{2}+f_{4})}{\partial r}-\frac{l+1}{r}(f_{2}+g_{2}+f_{4})+\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{2}+g_{2})+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}-\overline{M}\right)(f_{2}-g_{2}+g_{4})=0,

(707b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{2}-g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}g_{4})}{\partial r}-\frac{l}{r}\left(f_{2}-g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}g_{4}\right)-\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{2}-g_{2})-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}+\overline{M}\right)\left(f_{2}+g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}f_{4}\right)=0,

(707c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(f_{2}+g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}f_{4})}{\partial r}+\frac{l+2}{r}\left(f_{2}+g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}f_{4}\right)+\frac{2\overline{k}}{r}(f_{2}+g_{2})+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}-\overline{M}\right)\left(f_{2}-g_{2}-\frac{l+2}{l+1}g_{4}\right)=0.

(707d)

For Eq. (

), we designate

\displaystyle F_{1}=f_{1}-g_{1}\,\,,\,\,F_{2}=f_{1}+g_{1}.

(709a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r}g_{3}+\frac{l+2}{r}F_{1}+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}+\overline{M}\right)f_{3}=0.

(710a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r}f_{3}-\frac{l+2}{r}F_{2}-\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}-\overline{E}-\overline{M}\right)g_{3}=0.

(710b)

Firstly, we consider the behavior of

. For Eq. (

(

is real number), and require the solution is a bound state,
we then get

, i.e.

, and

\displaystyle\varepsilon=\sqrt{\overline{M}^{2}-\overline{E}^{2}}=\frac{1}{\hbar c}\sqrt{M^{2}c^{4}-E^{2}},

For Eq. (

(716a)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F_{2}}{\partial r}-\frac{2(\overline{k}-1)}{r}F_{2}-\frac{l+1}{r}f_{3}-\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}F_{1}=0,

(716b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{3}}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r}f_{3}-\frac{l+2}{r}F_{2}-\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}g_{3}=0,

(716c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{3}}{\partial r}+\frac{1}{r}g_{3}+\frac{l+2}{r}F_{1}+\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{r}f_{3}=0.

(716d)

If we designate

,

,

\displaystyle\nu d_{3}-\overline{\kappa}d_{4}-(l+2)d_{2}=0,

\displaystyle(l+2)d_{1}+\overline{\kappa}d_{3}+\nu d_{4}=0.

(717)

We can get the solution of
\nu
as follows

\displaystyle\nu=\pm\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\overline{k}-1+\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}},

\displaystyle{\rm or}\;\;\;\;\nu=\pm\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\overline{k}-1-\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}}.

(or

tends to 0), and

\displaystyle\nu=\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\overline{k}-1+\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}}.

(719)

Finally, we designate

\displaystyle F_{1}=\frac{1}{\mu}h_{1}(r)\,e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1},\;\;\;\;F_{2}=h_{2}(r)\,e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1},

\displaystyle f_{3}=h_{3}(r)\,e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1},\;\;\;\;g_{3}=\frac{1}{\mu}h_{4}(r)\,e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1},

(720)

where

(

) are functions of

. Then we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial h_{1}}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{2\overline{k}+\nu-1}{r}-\varepsilon\right)h_{1}+\frac{l+1}{r}h_{4}+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}\mu}{r}+\varepsilon\right)h_{2}=0,

(721a)

(721b)

(721c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial h_{4}}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu}{r}-\varepsilon\right)h_{4}+\frac{l+2}{r}h_{1}+\left(\frac{\overline{\kappa}\mu}{r}+\varepsilon\right)h_{3}=0.

(721d)

We continue to designate

\displaystyle h_{1}+h_{2}=A,\,\,\,\,h_{1}-h_{2}=B,\,\,\,\,h_{3}+h_{4}=C,\,\,\,\,h_{3}-h_{4}=D,\,\,\,\,\overline{p}=\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{2}\left(\mu+\frac{1}{\mu}\right),\,\,\,\,\overline{q}=\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{2}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right),

(722)

and then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial A}{\partial r}+\frac{\nu+\overline{q}}{r}A+\frac{2\overline{k}-1-\overline{p}}{r}B-\frac{l+1}{r}D=0,

(723a)

(723b)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial C}{\partial r}+\frac{\nu+\overline{q}}{r}C+\frac{l+2}{r}B+\frac{\overline{p}}{r}D=0,

(723c)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial D}{\partial r}+\left(\frac{\nu-\overline{q}}{r}-2\varepsilon\right)D-\frac{l+2}{r}A-\frac{\overline{p}}{r}C=0.

(723d)

If we assume
A=C_{1}C
and
B=C_{2}D
, it should satisfy

\displaystyle(l+2)C_{1}C_{2}+\overline{p}\,C_{1}-(2\overline{k}-1-\overline{p})C_{2}+l+1=0,

(724a)

\displaystyle(l+2)C_{1}C_{2}+(2\overline{k}-1+\overline{p})C_{1}+\overline{p}\,C_{2}+l+1=0.

(724b)

Based on which, we can attain

\displaystyle C_{1}=-C_{2}.

(725)

Then we have

\displaystyle(l+2)C_{1}^{2}-(2\overline{k}-1)C_{1}-(l+1)=0,

(726)

which leads to

\displaystyle C_{1}=\frac{(2\overline{k}-1)\pm\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2(l+2)}.

(727)

So if we designate

\displaystyle C=c_{0}+c_{1}r+\dots+c_{n}r^{n}+\dots,

\displaystyle D=d_{0}+d_{1}r+\dots+d_{n}r^{n}+\dots,

(728)

based on Eq. (

) and Eq. (

), we have

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu+\overline{q})c_{n+1}+\left\{-(l+2)C_{1}+\overline{p}\right\}d_{n+1}=0,

(729)

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu-\overline{q})d_{n+1}-2\varepsilon\,d_{n}-[(l+2)C_{1}+\overline{p}]c_{n+1}=0.

(730)

As a result,

\displaystyle d_{n+1}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+\nu)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(l+2)^{2}{C_{1}}^{2}}d_{n}.

(731)

Because when
\overline{k}=0
,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}(n+1+\nu)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(l+2)^{2}{C_{1}}^{2}=(n+1)(n+2\nu+1),}

(732)

thus
C_{1}
should be

\displaystyle C_{1}=\frac{l+1}{l+2}.

(733)

So we choose

\displaystyle C_{1}=\frac{(2\overline{k}-1)+\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2(l+2)},

(734)

which causes

\displaystyle d_{n+1}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+\nu)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(l+2)^{2}{C_{1}}^{2}}d_{n}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}d_{n}.

(735)

In addition, from

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu+\overline{q})c_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}(n+\nu+\overline{q})c_{n},

(736)

one has

\displaystyle c_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}c_{n}.

(737)

As a result, the coefficients are

\displaystyle A=C_{1}T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle B=-C_{1}T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle C=T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle D=T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

(738)

and

\displaystyle f_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)A+\left(\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)B\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}

(739a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)C_{1}T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)-\left(\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)C_{1}T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

\displaystyle g_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left\{\left(-\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)A-\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)B\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}

(739b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{\left(-\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)C_{1}T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)+\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)C_{1}T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

\displaystyle f_{3}=\frac{1}{2}\left\{C+D\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)+T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

(739c)

\displaystyle g_{3}=\frac{1}{2\mu}\left\{C-D\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2\mu}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{T_{1}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)-T_{2}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\}.

(739d)

Here
T_{1}
and

is the pending constant.

\displaystyle C^{\prime}=c^{\prime}_{0}+c^{\prime}_{1}r+\dots+c^{\prime}_{n}r^{n}+\dots,

(759)

based on Eq. (

754d
), we have

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu+\overline{q})c^{\prime}_{n+1}+\left\{-(l+2)C^{\prime}_{1}+\overline{p}\right\}d^{\prime}_{n+1}=0

(760)

and

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu-\overline{q})d^{\prime}_{n+1}-2ad^{\prime}_{n}-[(l+1)C^{\prime}_{1}+\overline{p}]c^{\prime}_{n+1}=0.

(761)

As a result,

\displaystyle d^{\prime}_{n+1}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+\nu)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(l+1)^{2}{C_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}}d^{\prime}_{n}.

(762)

Because when
\overline{k}=0
,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}(n+1+\nu)^{2}+\overline{\kappa}^{2}-(l+1)^{2}{C_{1}^{\prime}}^{2}=(n+1)(n+2\nu+1),}

(763)

C_{1}
should be

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\prime}=1.

(764)

So we choose

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{(2\overline{k}-1)+\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2(l+1)}.

(765)

It causes

\displaystyle d^{\prime}_{n+1}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+\nu)^{2}+k^{\prime 2}-(l+2)^{2}{C^{\prime}_{1}}^{2}}d^{\prime}_{n}=2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}d^{\prime}_{n}.

(766)

In addition, we have

\displaystyle(n+1+\nu+\overline{q})c^{\prime}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+1+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}(n+\nu+\overline{q})c^{\prime}_{n},

(767)

i.e.,

\displaystyle c^{\prime}_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\,\frac{n+\nu+\overline{q}}{(n+1+2\nu)(n+1)}c^{\prime}_{n}.

(768)

As a result,

\displaystyle A^{\prime}=T_{1}^{\prime}C_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle B=-C_{1}^{\prime}T_{2}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle C^{\prime}=T_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

\displaystyle D^{\prime}=T_{2}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r),

(769)

and

\displaystyle f_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left\{\left(-\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)A^{\prime}+\left(-\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)B^{\prime}\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}

(770a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)T_{1}^{\prime}C_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)+\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)C_{1}^{\prime}T_{2}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

\displaystyle g_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)A^{\prime}-\left(-\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)B^{\prime}\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}

(770b)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\mu}+1\right)T_{1}^{\prime}C_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)-\left(\frac{1}{\mu}-1\right)T_{2}^{\prime}C_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

\displaystyle f_{4}=\dfrac{1}{2}\left\{C^{\prime}+D^{\prime}\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{T_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)+T_{2}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

(770c)

\displaystyle g_{4}=-\dfrac{1}{2\mu}\left\{C^{\prime}-D^{\prime}\right\}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{2\mu}e^{-\varepsilon r}r^{\nu-1}\left\{T_{1}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q};2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)-T_{2}^{\prime}F(\nu+\overline{q}+1;2\nu+1;2\varepsilon r)\right\},

(770d)

where
T_{1}^{\prime}
and
T_{2}^{\prime}
is the pending constant.

When

\displaystyle\nu+\overline{q}=-N,\;\;\;(N=0,1,2,\dots),

(771)

A\,,\,B\,,\,C\,,\,D
and
A^{\prime}\,,\,B^{\prime}\,,\,C^{\prime}\,,\,D^{\prime}
become polynomials with respect to
r
.
Based on

\displaystyle N+\nu+\overline{q}=0,

\displaystyle\overline{q}=\frac{\overline{\kappa}}{2}\left(\mu-\frac{1}{\mu}\right),

\displaystyle\mu=\sqrt{\frac{Mc^{2}+E}{Mc^{2}-E}},

(772)

one can obtain the energy spectrum of the bound state as

\displaystyle E=\frac{mc^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{\overline{\kappa}^{2}}{(N+s)^{2}}}},\;\;\;\;\;(N=0,1,2,\dots),

(773)

i.e.,

\displaystyle E=\frac{mc^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\dfrac{q^{2}\kappa^{2}}{\hbar^{2}c^{2}}\dfrac{1}{(N+s)^{2}}}},\;\;\;\;\;(N=0,1,2,\dots).

(774)

When
k
tends to 0 (i.e., there is no spin vector potential
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
), the parameter

\displaystyle\nu=\sqrt{\left(\frac{2\overline{k}-1+\sqrt{(2\overline{k}-1)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}}.

(775)

reduces to
\nu=\sqrt{(l+1)^{2}-\overline{\kappa}^{2}}
. In this case, Eq. (
774
) naturally reduces to Eq. (
486
),
which is the energy of the usual relativistic hydrogen-like atom.

Remark 21
.

The Relation between the Fine Structure Constant
\alpha_{\rm e}
and the Nuclear Charge Number

Z
Modified by the Spin Vector Potential.

Due to

\displaystyle\frac{qk}{2c}=\overline{k},\;\;\;\;\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}=\overline{\kappa},

(776)

From Eq. (
775
) we can have

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(\frac{\dfrac{qk}{c}-1+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}}.

(777)

As a square-root, one should have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dfrac{qk}{c}-1+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)^{2}+4(l+1)(l+2)}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}\geq 0,

(778)

for any quantum numbers
l=0,1,2,\dots
. By considering the minimum
l_{\rm min}=0
, one has

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\dfrac{qk}{c}-1+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)^{2}+8}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{q\kappa}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}\geq 0.

(779)

By considering
q=-e
and
\kappa=Q=Ze
, one has

\displaystyle\left(\frac{-\dfrac{ek}{c}-1+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{ek}{c}+1\right)^{2}+8}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{Ze^{2}}{\hbar c}\right)^{2}\geq 0.

(780)

Due to

\displaystyle\alpha_{\rm e}=\dfrac{e^{2}}{\hbar c}\approx\dfrac{1}{137.0359}\approx\dfrac{1}{137},

(781)

one has

\displaystyle\left(\frac{-\dfrac{ek}{c}-1+\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{ek}{c}+1\right)^{2}+8}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(Z\alpha_{\rm e}\right)^{2}\geq 0,

(782)

i.e.,

\displaystyle Z\leq\frac{\sqrt{\left(\dfrac{ek}{c}+1\right)^{2}+8}-\left(\dfrac{ek}{c}+1\right)}{2}\,\dfrac{1}{\alpha_{\rm e}}.

(783)

This is the modified relation between the fine structure constant
\alpha_{\rm e}
and
Z
by considering the spin vector potential.

Discussion.—
For
k=0
(or
k
tends to 0), we recover the form result, i.e.,

\displaystyle Z\leq\dfrac{1}{\alpha_{\rm e}}\approx 137.0359.

(784)

As we have mentioned in the previous section, based on this finding, Richard Feynman asserted that the biggest number of

Z
in the periodic table of elements is
Z=137
.

For
k>0
. When
k
increases, the right-hand-side of Eq. (
783
) decreases, i.e.,
Z
cannot reach 137 any more. This means
that the spin-dependent Coulomb interaction can further suppress the the biggest number of
Z
.
At present, the biggest number
Z
listed in the periodic table of elements is
Z=118
(i.e., the element
{\rm Oq}
). Why scientists cannot create
the element with
Z>118
at present? A possible reason is due to the existence of the spin-dependent Coulomb interaction (with
k>0
).

Suppose
k>0
, let us focus on Eq. (
782
). For convenience, we denote
(e/c)k
by
k_{\rm cr}
. By solving

\displaystyle\left(\frac{-(k_{\rm cr}+1)+\sqrt{\left(k_{\rm cr}+1\right)^{2}+8}}{2}\right)^{2}-\left(Z\alpha_{\rm e}\right)^{2}=0,

(785)

we obtain

\displaystyle k_{\rm cr}=\dfrac{1-Z\alpha_{\rm e}-Z^{2}\,\alpha_{\rm e}^{2}}{Z\alpha_{\rm e}}.

(786)

In Table
2
, we have listed the relation between
Z
and
k_{\rm cr}
. For a fixed
Z
(e.g.,
Z=100
), if
(e/c)k
exceeds
k_{\rm cr}
, (e.g.,
k_{\rm cr}=1.0110
), then the atom system will become unstable.

VI.2.3
The case :
\kappa_{1}\neq 0
and
\kappa_{3}\neq 0

The Dirac-type Hamiltonian is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+q\varphi=\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\left(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\boxed{q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S_{2}})}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

(787)

However, it seems that the eigen-problem cannot be exactly solved.
Alternatively, one may re-write the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}+\mathcal{H}_{I},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+q\varphi=\vec{\alpha_{1}}\cdot\left(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)+\beta_{1}Mc^{2}+\boxed{q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S_{2}})}+\dfrac{q\kappa}{r},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boxed{q\kappa_{1}(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S_{2}})},

(788)

where the term
\mathcal{H}_{I}
is treated as an additional interaction based on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Generally, we can apply the perturbation theory method to investigate the energy spectrum of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
, which we shall study in the future work.

VII
Discussion

VII.1
From Dirac’s Equation to Pauli’s Equation and Schrödinger’s Equation

Dirac’s equation is a fundamental equation in relativistic quantum mechanics. In the non-relativistic limit, it can reduce firstly to Pauli’s equation and then to Schrödinger’s equation. In the following, we make a brief review for the procedure of such reductions.

Dirac’s Hamiltonian with vector potential
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
and scalar potential
\varphi
is given by

\mathcal{H}_{\rm Dirac}=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi,

(789)

where
M
is the mass of particle,
q
is the charge of the particle, and

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}-\frac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}

(790)

is the canonical momentum.

Then Dirac’s equation reads

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\Psi=\mathcal{H}_{\rm Dirac}\Psi,

(791)

where
\Psi
is the time-dependent wavefunction. Next we study the Dirac equation in the representation of

\Psi=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix},

(792)

then Eq. (
791
) becomes

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}=c\,\begin{bmatrix}0&\vec{\sigma}\\
\vec{\sigma}&0\end{bmatrix}\cdot\vec{\Pi}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix}\openone&0\\
0&-\openone\end{bmatrix}\,Mc^{2}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}+q\,\varphi\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix},

(793)

i.e.,

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\chi}\\
\tilde{\eta}\end{bmatrix}+Mc^{2}\,\openone\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
-\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}+q\,\varphi\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}.

(794)

Now, we consider the non-relativistic approximation of Dirac’s equation. In the case of low energy,
the term involving
Mc^{2}
is far larger than other ones. Then a part of time factor can be separated as follows.

\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\eta}\\
\tilde{\chi}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t},

(795)

thus Eq. (
794
) is transformed as

{\rm i}\hbar\Biggl\{\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}\Biggr\}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t}+{\rm i}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}\hbar\Bigl(\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t}\Bigr)=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\begin{bmatrix}\chi\\
\eta\end{bmatrix}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t}+Mc^{2}\openone\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
-\chi\end{bmatrix}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t}+q\,\varphi\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{Mc^{2}}{\hbar}t},

(796)

i.e.,

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}+Mc^{2}\openone\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\begin{bmatrix}\chi\\
\eta\end{bmatrix}+Mc^{2}\openone\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
-\chi\end{bmatrix}+q\,\varphi\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix},

(797)

i.e.,

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,}{\partial t}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\begin{bmatrix}\chi\\
\eta\end{bmatrix}+Mc^{2}\openone\begin{bmatrix}0\\
-2\,\chi\end{bmatrix}+q\,\varphi\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\chi\end{bmatrix}.

(798)

For the second row of Eq. (
798
), we have

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,\chi}{\partial t}=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\eta-2\,Mc^{2}\openone\chi+q\,\varphi\,\chi.

(799)

Since

\displaystyle Mc^{2}\,\chi\gg{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,\chi}{\partial t},

\displaystyle Mc^{2}\,\chi\gg q\,\phi\,\chi,

(800)

then from Eq. (
799
) we arrive at

c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\eta=2\,Mc^{2}\openone\chi,

(801)

which means

\chi=\dfrac{c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})}{2\,Mc^{2}}\eta,

(802)

i.e., the wavefunction
\eta
can be connected to the wavefunction
\chi
through the relation (
802
).

After that, for the first row, we obtain

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,\eta}{\partial t}=c(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\chi+q\,\varphi\,\eta,

(803)

i.e.,

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,\eta}{\partial t}=\dfrac{1}{2M}(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})\eta+q\,\varphi\,\eta.

(804)

Because

(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\Pi})=\vec{\Pi}^{2}+{\rm i}\vec{\sigma}\cdot(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}),

(805)

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\left(\vec{p}-\frac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)\times\left(\vec{p}-\frac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)\right]=\frac{q}{c}\left[\vec{p}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}-\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{p}\right]+\frac{q^{2}}{c^{2}}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}

(806)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{q}{c}\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}+\frac{q^{2}}{c^{2}}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}},

then for the spin vector potential given in Eq. (
164
), i.e.,

\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},

(807)

one can have

\displaystyle\nabla\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}=-2k\,\dfrac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\vec{r},\;\;\;\;\;\;\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}={\rm i}\hbar\;k^{2}\;\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r},

\displaystyle{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)={\rm i}\left[-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{q}{c}\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}+\frac{q^{2}}{c^{2}}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]={\rm i}\left\{-{\rm i}\hbar\frac{q}{c}\left[-2k\,\dfrac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\vec{r}\right]+\frac{q^{2}}{c^{2}}\left[{\rm i}\hbar\;k^{2}\;\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle={\rm i}\left\{{\rm i}\hbar\frac{qk}{c}\left[2+\frac{qk}{c}\right]\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}\right\}=-\hbar\frac{qk}{c}\left[2+\frac{qk}{c}\right]\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r},

(808)

and the “magnetic” filed

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{\nabla}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}-{\rm i}\,g\left(\vec{\mathcal{A}}\times\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)=\left[-2k\,\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{r^{4}}\vec{r}\right]-{\rm i}\,g\left[{\rm i}\hbar\;k^{2}\;\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}\right]=k(g\hbar k-2)\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}.

(809)

Case (i).—
For the type-I spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
k=0
and
g=0
, thus
\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0
.
In this case, one has
\vec{\Pi}^{2}=\vec{p}^{\,2}
, then Eq. (
804
) becomes

{\rm i}\hbar\dfrac{\partial\,\eta}{\partial t}=\left(\dfrac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+q\,\varphi\right)\eta,

(810)

which is just Schrödinger’s equation without vector potential in non-relativistic quantum mechanics, with Schrödinger’s Hamiltonian

\mathcal{H}_{\rm Schr}=\dfrac{\vec{p}^{\,2}}{2M}+q\,\varphi.

(811)

Case (ii).—
For the type-II spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
g\hbar k=1
. In this case, one has

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{(g\hbar k-2)}{-\hbar\dfrac{q}{c}\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]}\left[{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)\right]=\dfrac{1}{\hbar\dfrac{q}{c}\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]}\left[{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)\right]=\dfrac{1}{\gamma}\,\left[{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)\right],

(812)

with

\displaystyle\gamma=\hbar\dfrac{q}{c}\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right].

(813)

In this case, Eq. (
804
) becomes

{\rm i}\hbar\frac{\partial\eta}{\partial t}=\left[\frac{\vec{\Pi}^{2}+\gamma\,\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}}{2M}+q\varphi\right]\eta,

(814)

which is just Pauli’s equation in the non-relativistic quantum mechanics. Based on Eq. (
814
) one may extract the Pauli Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm Pauli}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\vec{\Pi}^{2}+\gamma\,\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}}{2M}+q\varphi\right]=\dfrac{1}{2M}\Bigl(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{A}\Bigr)^{2}+\dfrac{\gamma}{2M}\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}+q\,\varphi.

(815)

When
\gamma
is very small and can be neglected, then Pauli’s equation reduces further to Schrödinger’s equation in non-relativistic quantum mechanics. The corresponding Schrödinger Hamiltonian with spin potential is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm Schr}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\vec{\Pi}^{2}}{2M}+q\varphi=\dfrac{1}{2M}\Bigl(\vec{p}-\dfrac{q}{c}\vec{A}\Bigr)^{2}+q\,\varphi.

(816)

Remark 22
.

The Tensor Interaction.
The relation between Pauli’s Hamiltonian and Schrödinger’s Hamiltonian is

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm Pauli}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm Schr}+\boxed{\dfrac{\gamma}{2M}\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}}.

(817)

Namely, in comparison to Schrödinger’s Hamiltonian, there is an extra term in Pauli’s Hamiltonian, which is

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm extra}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{\gamma}{2\,m}\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}.

(818)

This extra term can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rm extra}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V(r)\mathcal{T}_{12},

(819)

where

\displaystyle V(r)\propto\dfrac{\gamma}{2M}\dfrac{1}{r^{2}},

(820)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{12}=\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{\sigma}_{1}\right)\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{\sigma}_{2}\right)}{r^{2}}

(821)

represents a tensor interaction between two spins. Here the “
\vec{\sigma}\;
” in
\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\mathcal{B}}
is
\vec{\sigma}_{1}

(representing the spin of the first particle,i.e.,
\vec{S}_{1}=\dfrac{\hbar}{2}\vec{\sigma}
), and

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k(g\hbar k-2)\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}=-k\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}_{2}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}=-\dfrac{\hbar k}{2}\frac{\vec{r}\cdot\vec{\sigma}_{2}}{r^{4}}\;\vec{r}

(822)

is the “magnetic” field induced by the spin of the second particle. The physical meaning of the tensor interaction
\mathcal{T}_{12}
is

the dipole-dipole spin interaction
between two particles, which is very common in nuclear physics
1999Flugge
. Based on the above derivation,
one may find that the dipole-dipole spin interaction is a natural result of Yang-Mills equations. More explicitly, one can predict the
spin-dependent Coulomb interaction based on Yang-Mills equations, by considering the non-relativistic approximation of Dirac’s equation,
from the spin-dependent Coulomb interaction one can naturally obtain the dipole-dipole spin interaction.
\blacksquare

VII.2
From Heisenberg’s Equation to Spin Forces

VII.2.1
Heisenberg’s Equation

Heisenberg’s equation of motion is a fundamental equation in quantum mechanics, which is given by

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{H}(t)}{{\rm d}\,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left(\Bigl[\hat{\mathcal{O}}(t),\ \mathcal{H}\Bigr]\right)_{H}+\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H},

(823)

where “
\mathcal{H}
” denotes the Hamiltonian of physical system, and “
H
” represents the Heisenberg picture.

Remark 23
.

Derivation of Heisenberg’s Equation.
Let
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
be a physical observable, which is a time-independent hermitian operator. The time-dependent operator
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{H}(t)
is defined through

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{H}(t)=U^{\dagger}(t)\hat{\mathcal{O}}U(t),

(824)

where

\displaystyle U(t)={\rm e}^{\frac{-{\rm i}}{\hbar}\mathcal{H}t}

(825)

is a unitary evolution operator, and

\displaystyle U^{\dagger}(t)={\rm e}^{\frac{{\rm i}}{\hbar}\mathcal{H}t},

\displaystyle U(t)U^{\dagger}(t)=U^{\dagger}(t)U(t)=\mathbb{I},

(826)

with
\mathbb{I}
being the identity operator.

From Eq. (
825
) one can have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial U(t)}{\partial t}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\mathcal{H}U(t)=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}U(t)\mathcal{H},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial U^{\dagger}(t)}{\partial t}=-\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\mathcal{H}U^{\dagger}(t)=-\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}U^{\dagger}(t)\mathcal{H},

(827)

which yields

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{H}(t)}{{\rm d}\,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\,\left[U^{\dagger}(t)\hat{\mathcal{O}}U(t)\right]}{{\rm d}\,t}

(828)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{\partial\,\hat{U}^{\dagger}}{\partial\,t}\hat{\mathcal{O}}U+\hat{U}^{\dagger}\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}U+\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{\mathcal{O}}\dfrac{\partial\,U}{\partial\,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\hat{U}^{\dagger}\mathcal{H}U\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{\mathcal{O}}U+\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H}+\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{\mathcal{O}}U\hat{U}^{\dagger}\mathcal{H}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\mathcal{H}\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{\mathcal{O}}U+\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H}+\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\hat{U}^{\dagger}\hat{\mathcal{O}}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left[\mathcal{H}\hat{\mathcal{O}}(t)-\hat{\mathcal{O}}(t)\mathcal{H}\right]+\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left(\Bigl[\hat{\mathcal{O}}(t),\ H\Bigr]\right)_{H}+\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H}.

Moreover, when
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
does not apparently contain time
t
, one has

\left(\dfrac{\partial\,\hat{\mathcal{O}}}{\partial\,t}\right)_{H}=0.

(829)

This ends the derivation.
\blacksquare

VII.2.2
Spin Force: The Case of Non-Relativistic Quantum Mechanics

Firstly, for simplicity let us consider the case of non-relativistic quantum mechanics. In such a case, the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
in Heisenberg’ equation (
823
) is chosen as the Schrödinger-type Hamiltonian, which is given by

\mathcal{H}=\dfrac{1}{2M}\vec{\Pi}^{2}+q\varphi,

(830)

with

\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}-\frac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}.

(831)

Here
M
is the mass of the particle,
\vec{p}
is the usual linear momentum operator,
\vec{\Pi}
is the canonical momentum operator,
q
is the “charge”,
c
is the speed of light in vacuum,
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
is the vector potential, and
\varphi
is the scalar potential.

We now select the physical observable
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
as the position operator
\vec{r}
. Based on Heisenberg’s equation, we obtain
the “velocity” operator as

\displaystyle\vec{v}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\,\vec{r}}{{\rm d}\,t}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\left[\vec{r},\mathcal{H}\right]+\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{r}}{\partial\,t}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\left[\vec{r},\mathcal{H}\right]

(832)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{M}\left(\vec{p}-\frac{q}{c}\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right)\equiv\dfrac{1}{M}\;\vec{\Pi}.

Here we have assumed that the vector potential
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
does not depend on
\vec{p}
, thus
\vec{r}
and
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
are mutually commutative.

Furthermore, we can have the “acceleration” operator as

\displaystyle\vec{a}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{{\rm d}\,\vec{v}}{{\rm d}\,t}=\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\left[\vec{v},\mathcal{H}\right]+\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{v}}{\partial\,t}

(833)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2{\rm i}\,\hbar\,M^{2}}\left[\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]+\dfrac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\left[\vec{v},\ q\varphi\right]+\dfrac{1}{M}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\Pi}}{\partial\,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2{\rm i}\,\hbar\,M^{2}}\left[\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]+\dfrac{q}{{\rm i}\,\hbar\,M}\left[\vec{\Pi},\ \varphi\right]-\dfrac{q}{Mc}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2{\rm i}\,\hbar\,M^{2}}\left[\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]-\dfrac{q}{M}(\vec{\nabla}\varphi)-\dfrac{q^{2}}{{\rm i}\,\hbar\,Mc}\left[\vec{\mathcal{A}},\ \varphi\right]-\dfrac{q}{Mc}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}.

Because

\displaystyle\left[{\Pi}_{z},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\Pi_{z},\ \Pi_{x}\right]\Pi_{x}+\Pi_{x}\left[\Pi_{z},\ \Pi_{x}\right]+\Pi_{y}\left[\Pi_{z},\ \Pi_{y}\right]+\left[\Pi_{z},\ \Pi_{y}\right]\Pi_{y}.

(834)

If we designate

\displaystyle[\Pi_{a},\Pi_{b}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{abc}G_{c},

(835)

with
\epsilon_{abc}
is the Levi-Civita symbol, or

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}={\rm i}\hbar\,\vec{G},

(836)

then we can get

\left[\vec{\Pi}_{z},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]={\rm i}\hbar\left[\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{G}\right)_{z}-\left(\vec{G}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)_{z}\right],

(837)

which means

\displaystyle\left[\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{G}\right)-\left(\vec{G}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)\right]={\rm i}\hbar\,{M}\left[\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{G}\right)-\left(\vec{G}\times\vec{v}\right)\right].

(838)

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\vec{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{M}\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{G}-\vec{G}\times\vec{v}\right)+\dfrac{q}{M}\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr].

(839)

Case (i).—
For the type-I spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
k=0
,
g=0
. In this case, one has
\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0
,
\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}
,
\vec{G}=0
, then

\displaystyle\vec{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{q}{M}\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi=-\dfrac{q}{M}\vec{\nabla}\,\left[\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right],

(840)

According to the form of “force” (defined in classical mechanics), we have

\displaystyle\vec{F}=M\vec{a}=-q\vec{\nabla}\,\left[\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right].

(841)

This the spin force in non-relativistic quantum mechanics, and
\vec{F}
reduces to the usual Coulomb force when
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=0
and
\kappa_{3}=Q
.

Case (ii).—
For the type-II spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
g\hbar k=1
. In this case, one has

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{A}}=k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}},\;\;\;\;\;\varphi=\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}\,\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}.

(842)

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\gamma}\,\left[{\rm i}\left(\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}\right)\right]=\dfrac{{\rm i}}{\gamma}\,{\rm i}\hbar\vec{G}=-\dfrac{\hbar}{\gamma}\,\vec{G},\;\;\;\;\;\gamma=\hbar\dfrac{q}{c}\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right].

(843)

Then we have

\displaystyle\vec{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{M}\dfrac{1}{2}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{G}-\vec{G}\times\vec{v}\right)+\dfrac{q}{M}\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr]

(844)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{M}\dfrac{1}{2}\dfrac{\gamma}{\hbar}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+\dfrac{q}{M}\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{q}{M}\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{1}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+\dfrac{q}{M}\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr],

and the spin force as

\displaystyle\vec{F}=M\vec{a}=-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr].

(845)

Due to the “electric” field

\displaystyle\vec{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\varphi-{\rm i}\,g\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right],

(846)

one has

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\biggl[-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-{\rm i}\,g\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr]+q\biggl[{\rm i}\,g\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr]

(847)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+q\biggl[{\rm i}\,\dfrac{1}{\hbar k}\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+\dfrac{q}{\hbar k}\left(1-\dfrac{qk}{c}\right){\rm i}\,\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right].

We come to calculate the following commutator

\displaystyle{\rm i}\,\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\,\left[\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}\,\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r},\ k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}\right]={\rm i}\,\left[\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r}\,\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right),\ k\frac{\vec{r}\times\vec{S}}{r^{2}}\right]={\rm i}\,\dfrac{k\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},\ \vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right].

(848)

Because

\displaystyle\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},\ \left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)_{z}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z},xS_{y}-yS_{x}\right]

(849)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z},xS_{y}\right]-\left[xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z},yS_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[xS_{x}+zS_{z},xS_{y}\right]-\left[yS_{y}+zS_{z},yS_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{2}\left[S_{x},S_{y}\right]+zx\left[S_{z},S_{y}\right]-y^{2}\left[S_{y},S_{x}\right]-zy\left[S_{z},S_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x^{2}+y^{2}){\rm i}\hbar S_{z}-zx\,{\rm i}\hbar S_{x}-zy\,{\rm i}\hbar S_{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(x^{2}+y^{2}+z^{2}){\rm i}\hbar S_{z}-{\rm i}\hbar(xS_{x}+yS_{y}+zS_{z})z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[r^{2}S_{z}-\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)z\right],

then we have

\displaystyle\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},\ \left(\vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\hbar\left[r^{2}\vec{S}-\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\vec{r}\right],

(850)

which yields

\displaystyle{\rm i}\,\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm i}\,\dfrac{k\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}\left[\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S},\ \vec{r}\times\vec{S}\right]=-\dfrac{\hbar k\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}\left[r^{2}\vec{S}-\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\vec{r}\right].

(851)

Then one has the spin force as

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+\dfrac{q}{\hbar k}\left(1-\dfrac{qk}{c}\right){\rm i}\,\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]

(852)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+\dfrac{q}{\hbar k}\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)\dfrac{\hbar k\kappa_{1}}{r^{3}}\left[r^{2}\vec{S}-\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)\vec{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+q\kappa_{1}\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)\left[\dfrac{\vec{S}}{r}-\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{r^{3}}\vec{r}\right].

One may note that the spin force consists three parts: the first part represents the magnetic-like force (i.e., the Lorenz-like force), the second part represents the electric-like force, and the third part related to spin
S
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\vec{F}_{\rm magnetic}+\vec{F}_{\rm electric}+\vec{F}_{3},

(853)

with

\displaystyle\vec{F}_{\rm magnetic}=-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)\propto\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right),

\displaystyle\vec{F}_{\rm electric}=q\vec{\mathcal{E}},

\displaystyle\vec{F}_{3}=q\kappa_{1}\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)\left[\dfrac{\vec{S}}{r}-\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{r^{3}}\vec{r}\right].

(854a)

By the way, the third force comes from the commutator
\left[\varphi,\ \vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]
, which becomes zero in classical mechanics.

VII.2.3
Spin Force: The Case of Relativistic Quantum Mechanics

Secondly, let us consider the case of relativistic quantum mechanics. In such a case, the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}
in Heisenberg’ equation (
823
) is chosen as the Dirac-type Hamiltonian, which is given by

\mathcal{H}=c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi}+\beta Mc^{2}+q\varphi,

(855)

where

\displaystyle\vec{\alpha}=\begin{bmatrix}0&\vec{\sigma}\\
\vec{\sigma}&0\end{bmatrix},\;\;\;\;\;\beta=\begin{bmatrix}\openone&0\\
0&-\openone\end{bmatrix},

(856)

are the Dirac matrices,
\openone
is the
2\times 2
identity matrix,
\vec{\sigma}=(\sigma_{x},\sigma_{y},\sigma_{z})
is the vector of Pauli matrices. Because the vector potential
\vec{\mathcal{A}}
and the scalar potential
\varphi
commute with Dirac’s matrices
\vec{\alpha}
and
\beta
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{A}_{j},\vec{\alpha}\right]=0,\;\;\;\left[\mathcal{A}_{j},\beta\right]=0,\;\;\;(j=x,y,z),

\displaystyle\left[\varphi,\vec{\alpha}\right]=0,\;\;\;\left[\varphi,\beta\right]=0,

(857)

then one easily derives the “velocity” operator as

\displaystyle\vec{v}:=\frac{d\vec{r}}{dt}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\left[\vec{r},\mathcal{H}\right]+\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{r}}{\partial\,t}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\,\hbar}\,c\,\vec{\alpha}\cdot\left[\vec{r},\vec{p}\right]=c\,\vec{\alpha}.

(858)

However, in special relativity, the mass of the particle is not a constant (i.e., it is velocity-dependent). Due to this reason, we shall attain the “force” operator directly through the momentum operator
\vec{\Pi}
as follows

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{d\vec{\Pi}}{dt}=\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left[\vec{\Pi},\mathcal{H}\right]+\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\Pi}}{\partial\,t}

(859)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left\{c\,\left[\vec{\Pi},\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi}\right]+q\left[\vec{\Pi},\varphi\right]\right\}-\frac{q}{c}\dfrac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}\left\{-c\,\left[\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}\right]+q\left[\vec{p},\varphi\right]-\frac{q^{2}}{c}\left[\vec{\mathcal{A}},\varphi\right]\right\}-\frac{q}{c}\dfrac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{{\rm i}\hbar}}\left\{-c\,\left[\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}\right]{-{\rm i}\hbar}\,q\vec{\nabla}\varphi{+}\frac{q^{2}}{c}[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]\right\}-\frac{q}{c}\frac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{-1}{{\rm i}\hbar}}\left\{c\,\left[\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}\right]{+{\rm i}\hbar}\,q\vec{\nabla}\varphi{-}\frac{q^{2}}{c}[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]\right\}-\frac{q}{c}\frac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}.

If we still designate

\displaystyle[\Pi_{a},\Pi_{b}]={\rm i}\hbar\,\epsilon_{abc}G_{c},

(860)

or

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}\times\vec{\Pi}={\rm i}\hbar\,\vec{G},

(861)

we then have

\displaystyle[\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi},{\Pi}_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\alpha_{x}\Pi_{x}+\alpha_{y}\Pi_{y}+\alpha_{z}\Pi_{z},{\Pi}_{z}]

(862)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{x}[\Pi_{x},{\Pi}_{z}]+\alpha_{y}[\Pi_{y},{\Pi}_{z}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\,\alpha_{x}G_{y}+{\rm i}\hbar\,\alpha_{y}G_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\,\left(\vec{\alpha}\times\vec{G}\right)_{z},

which means

\displaystyle[\vec{\alpha}\cdot\vec{\Pi},\vec{\Pi}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\rm i}\hbar\,\left(\vec{\alpha}\times\vec{G}\right).

(863)

After substituting Eq. (
863
) into Eq. (
859
), we have

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\frac{-1}{{\rm i}\hbar}}\left\{-{\rm i}\hbar\,c\,\left(\vec{\alpha}\times\vec{G}\right){+{\rm i}\hbar}\,q\vec{\nabla}\varphi{-}\frac{q^{2}}{c}[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]\right\}-\frac{q}{c}\frac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}

(864)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\,\left(\vec{\alpha}\times\vec{G}\right){-}\,q\vec{\nabla}\varphi{-\frac{{\rm i}}{\hbar}}\frac{q^{2}}{c}[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}]-\frac{q}{c}\frac{\partial\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{G}\right)+q\left\{-\frac{1}{c}\dfrac{\partial\,\vec{\mathcal{A}}}{\partial\,t}-\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi-\dfrac{q}{c}\dfrac{{i}}{\hbar}\left[\varphi,\vec{\mathcal{A}}\right]\right\}.

Case (i).—
For the type-I spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
k=0
,
g=0
. In this case, one has
\vec{\mathcal{A}}=0
,
\vec{\Pi}=\vec{p}
,
\vec{G}=0
, then

\displaystyle\vec{F}=-q\vec{\nabla}\,\varphi=-q\vec{\nabla}\,\left[\left(\dfrac{\kappa_{1}}{r^{2}}+\kappa_{2}\,r\right)\left(\hat{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa_{3}}{r}\right].

(865)

This the spin force in relativistic quantum mechanics, and
\vec{F}
reduces to the usual Coulomb force when
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=0
and
\kappa_{3}=Q
.

Case (ii).—
For the type-II spin-dependent Coulomb potential, one has known that
g\hbar k=1
. In this case, similarly one has

\displaystyle\vec{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[2+\dfrac{qk}{c}\right]\dfrac{q}{2c}\left(\vec{v}\times\vec{\mathcal{B}}-\vec{\mathcal{B}}\times\vec{v}\right)+q\vec{\mathcal{E}}+q\kappa_{1}\left(\dfrac{qk}{c}-1\right)\left[\dfrac{\vec{S}}{r}-\dfrac{\left(\vec{r}\cdot\vec{S}\right)}{r^{3}}\vec{r}\right],

(866)

which shares the same form with the non-relativistic case. The only difference is that
\vec{v}
commutes with
\vec{\mathcal{B}}
in the relativistic case (i.e.,
\left[\vec{v},\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right]=0
), while they do not commute in the non-relativistic case (i.e.,
\left[\vec{v},\vec{\mathcal{B}}\right]\neq 0
).
